P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right):=\left\langle f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\right)\mu\left(\mathrm{d}x_{1}\right)\cdots\mu\left(\mathrm{d}x_{n}\right)}}

(22)

with
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1,K}^{c}({\mathbb{R}})
,
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{3}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
and where
\mu^{n}
is the
n-
fold product measure of
\mu
. In Lemmas
28
and
3.7
we extend this result to the case of specific unbounded test functions.

For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, we denote by
\bm{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, the vector whose coordinates are all
1
and by
\Delta
the Laplacian operator on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. Let us introduce, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
\lambda>0
and
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
, the operator
B_{\lambda}^{(n)}
defined by

B_{\lambda}^{(n)}f(x):=\frac{1}{2}\Delta f(x)-2\lambda\left(\nabla f(x)\cdot\bm{1}\right)(x\cdot\bm{1}),\qquad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

(23)

Let us consider for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
for all
i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,n\right\}
,

•

\Phi_{i,j}:{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{n-1},{\mathbb{R}})
, with
i\neq j
, is the function obtained from
f
by inserting the variable
x_{i}
between
x_{j-1}
and
x_{j}
when
i<j
and by inserting the variable
x_{i-1}
between
x_{j-1}
and
x_{j}
when
i>j
:

\begin{aligned} \Phi_{i,j}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n-1}\right)&=f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{j-1},x_{i},x_{j},x_{j+1},\cdots,x_{n-1}\right)&\qquad i<j\\
\Phi_{i,j}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n-1}\right)&=f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{j-1},x_{i-1},x_{j},x_{j+1},\cdots,x_{n-1}\right)&\qquad i>j\end{aligned}\ .

(24)

•

K_{i,j}:{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}})\longrightarrow{\mathscr{C}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n+1},{\mathbb{R}})
is defined as

K_{i,j}f(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n},x_{n+1}):=\partial_{ij}^{2}f(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})x_{n+1}^{2}.

(25)

We recall from

[
18
,
Definition 2.8]
that the extended generator (in the sense of
Dynkin
, see (
27
) below)
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm{FVc}}^{\lambda}
of the centered
Fleming-Viot
process with resampling rate
\lambda
is defined for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, for any test functions
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
by

{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm{FVc}}^{\lambda}P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right)=\left\langle B_{\lambda}^{(n)}f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle+\lambda\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left[\left\langle\Phi_{i,j}f,\mu^{n-1}\right\rangle-\left\langle f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle\right]}+\lambda\sum_{i,j\,=\,1}^{n}{\left\langle K_{i,j}f,\mu^{n+1}\right\rangle}.

(26)

From

[
18
,
Definition 2.8]
and denoting

\widetilde{\Omega}:=\left\{X\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}\left(\left[0,+\infty\right),{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right.\forall T>0,\ \sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}{M_{2}\left(X_{t}\right)}<\infty\right\},

we recall that a probability measure
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}
on
\widetilde{\Omega}
is said to solve the
centered
Fleming-Viot
martingale problem for polynomials
with initial condition
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
, if the canonical process
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
on
\widetilde{\Omega}
satisfies
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}\left(X_{0}=\mu\right)=1
and, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
,

\widehat{M}_{t}^{P_{f,n}}:=P_{f,n}\left(X_{t}\right)-P_{f,n}\left(X_{0}\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{f,n}\left(X_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s}

(27)

is a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}-
martingale.

Lemma 3.4
.

The infinitesimal generator
{\mathcal{L}}^{K}
of the
{\mathbb{R}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times}\,{\mathcal{M}}_{1,K}^{c}({\mathbb{R}})-
valued
Markov
process
\left(z^{K},\mu^{K}\right)
given by
Proposition
3.1
, satisfies for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{3}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
, the following relations:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}^{K}P_{f,n}(z,\mu)

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta(z)m_{2}(z)}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm{FVc}}^{\lambda(z)}P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right)+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\times O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3}{2}}M_{2}\left(\mu\right)+\frac{M_{3}\left(\mu\right)}{K}\right)

where
\lambda(z):=\frac{b(z,z)}{\theta(z)m_{2}(z)}
. Moreover, for all
K,n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and test functions
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{3}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
, the process
\left(M_{t}^{K,P_{f,n}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
defined by

M_{t}^{K,P_{f,n}}:=P_{f,n}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)-P_{f,n}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}^{K}P_{f,n}\left(z_{s}^{K},\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\mathrm{d}s}

is a square integrale martingale started at
0
.

The proof of this result is given in Section
3.4
.

3.3.2
For some unbounded test functions

The following results are particular extensions of Lemma
3.4
when the test functions are no longer bounded but the processes are stopped at time
\check{\tau}^{K}
Then, we give in Lemmas
28
and
3.7

Doob
’s semi-martingale decomposition of moments of order
2
and those whose degree is
6
.

Proposition 3.5
.

For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{3}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
, the process
\left(M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{f,n}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
defined by

\displaystyle M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{f,n}}

\displaystyle:=P_{f,n}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)-P_{f,n}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)-\frac{K}{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)}}}

\displaystyle\times b\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K}\right)\left[P_{f\circ\tau_{-\frac{y-x}{K}},n}\left(\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right)-P_{f,n}(\mu)\right]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{K}{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\theta\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K}\right)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{m\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K},h\right)\mathrm{d}h}}}

\displaystyle\times\left[P_{f\circ\tau_{-\frac{h}{K^{\frac{3}{2}}}},n}\left(\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{x+\frac{h}{\sqrt{K}}}}{K}\right)-P_{f,n}(\mu)\right]\mathrm{d}s

is a bounded martingale.

Proof.

Thanks to the stopping time
\check{\tau}^{K}
,
\left(M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{f,n}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is bounded. Hence the martingality of this process follows from Lemma
3.4
. ∎

(a) Moment of order
2
.
The following result will be useful in Section
4
.

Lemma 3.6
.

For all
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, the process
\left(M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
defined in
Proposition
3.5
satisfies for all
t\geqslant 0

\displaystyle M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}

\displaystyle=M_{2}\left(\mu^{K}_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)-M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left\{2b\left(z_{s}^{K},z_{s}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)-\theta\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)m_{2}\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)\right\}\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{O\left(\frac{1}{K}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}M_{1}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3}{2}}M_{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s}

is a square integrable martingale with quadratic variation:

\displaystyle\left\langle M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right\rangle_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{b\left(z_{s}^{K},z_{s}^{K}\right)\left\{M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)-M_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right\}\mathrm{d}s}

(28)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{O\left(\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}}{K^{2}}+\frac{M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)}{K}+\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}M_{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)}{K}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}M_{5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s}.

Proof.

Step 1. Approximation of the
Doob
decomposition.
Note that for all
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

P_{{\rm{id}}^{2}\circ\tau_{-\frac{y-x}{K}},1}\left(\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right)-P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}(\mu)=\frac{1}{K}\left(y^{2}-x^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{K^{2}}(y-x)^{2}.

From
Taylor
’s formula, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{K}{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}s\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)b\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}y+z_{s}^{K}\right)}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 129.46011pt\times\left[P_{{\rm{id}}^{2}\circ\tau_{-\frac{y-x}{K}},1}\left(\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right)-P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}(\mu)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}{\left[-2b\left(z_{s}^{K},z_{s}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)+O\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3}{2}}M_{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s}

and

\displaystyle\frac{K}{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}s\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{m\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K},h\right)\theta\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K}\right)}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt\times\left[P_{{\rm{id}}^{2}\circ\tau_{-\frac{h}{K^{3/2}}},1}\left(\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{x+\frac{h}{\sqrt{K}}}}{K}\right)-P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}(\mu)\right]\mathrm{d}h

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}{\left[\theta\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)m_{2}\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{K}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}M_{1}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\right]\mathrm{d}s}.

We deduce the first announced result.

Step 2. Quadratic variation.
In similar way to the proof of Proposition
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right\rangle_{t}=\frac{K}{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}s\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu_{s}^{K}(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu_{s}^{K}(\mathrm{d}y)b\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}y+z_{s}^{K}\right)}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\left[\frac{1}{K}\left(y^{2}-x^{2}\right)-\frac{1}{K^{2}}(y-x)^{2}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{K}{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}{\mathrm{d}s\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu_{s}^{K}(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{m\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K},h\right)\theta\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z_{s}^{K}\right)}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\times\left[\frac{h^{2}}{K^{2}}-\frac{h^{2}}{K^{3}}+\frac{2xh}{K^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right]^{2}\mathrm{d}h.

The announced result follows from
Taylor
’s formula and straightforward computations. ∎

(b) Moments of degree
6
.
Let us denote for all
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\widetilde{M}_{\ell}(\mu):=\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{\ell},\mu\right\rangle
.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let us consider the functions
f,g:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
and
h:{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\to{\mathbb{R}}
respectively defined by
f(u,v):=u^{4}v^{2}
,
g(u,v):=u^{3}v^{3}
and
h(u,v,w):=u^{2}v^{2}w^{2}
. For all
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, the processes
\left(M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{6},1}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
,
\left(M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{f,2}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
,
\left(M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{g,2}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
and
\left(M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{h,3}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
satisfiy for all
t\geqslant 0

\displaystyle\bullet\ M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{6},1}}

\displaystyle=M_{6}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)-M_{6}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)+\frac{3}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left\{b\left(z_{s}^{K},z_{s}^{K}\right)\left[4M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right.\right.}

\displaystyle\left.\left.-10M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right]-5\theta\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)m_{2}\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right\}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{O\left(\frac{M_{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)}{\sqrt{K}}+\frac{1+M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)}{K}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}M_{5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right.},

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)}{K^{2}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3}{2}}M_{7}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\bullet\ M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{f,2}}

\displaystyle=M_{4}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)-M_{4}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left\{2b\left(z_{s}^{K},z_{s}^{K}\right)\left[6M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)-M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)+4\widetilde{M}_{3}^{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right.\right.}

\displaystyle\left.\left.-6M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right]-\theta\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)m_{2}\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)\left[6M_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)+M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right]\right\}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{O\left(\frac{M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)}{K^{3}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3}{2}}M_{7}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s},

\displaystyle\bullet\ M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{g,2}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{M}_{3}^{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)-\widetilde{M}_{3}^{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left\{b\left(z_{s}^{K},z_{s}^{K}\right)\left[-2M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right.\right.}

\displaystyle+\left.\left.14\widetilde{M}_{3}^{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)+12M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)-18M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right]\right\}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{O\left(\frac{M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)}{K^{3}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3}{2}}M_{7}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s},

\displaystyle\bullet\ M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{h,3}}

\displaystyle=M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)-M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left\{b\left(z_{s}^{K},z_{s}^{K}\right)\left[12M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right.\right.}

\displaystyle-\left.\left.6M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right]-3\theta\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)m_{2}\left(z_{s}^{K}\right)M_{4}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right\}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{O\left(\frac{M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)}{K^{3}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3}{2}}M_{7}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s}.

Proof.

The approximation proposed is obtained in a similar way to the proof of Lemma
28
∎

3.4
Proof of Lemma
3.4

From Proposition
3.1
, for the choice of test functions
\Phi(z,\mu):=P_{f,n}(\mu)
given by (
22
) and noting that for all
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\left\langle f,\left(\tau_{-\frac{y-x}{K}}\,\sharp\,\left[\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]\right)^{n}\right\rangle=\left\langle f\circ\tau_{-\frac{y-x}{K}},\left[\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{n}\right\rangle,

we obtain that
{\mathcal{L}}^{K}P_{f,n}(z,\mu)=\frac{K}{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\left(\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}^{K}+{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}^{K}\right)
where

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K}

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu\left(\mathrm{d}x\right)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu\left(\mathrm{d}y\right)b\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}y+z\right)}{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}}_{x,y}^{K}},

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}^{K}

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu\left(\mathrm{d}x\right)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\theta\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z\right)m\left(z+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x,h\right)}{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}}_{x,x+h/\sqrt{K}}^{K}\,\mathrm{d}h}

where
{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}}_{x,y}^{K}:=\left\langle f\circ\tau_{-\frac{y-x}{K}},\left[\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{n}\right\rangle-\left\langle f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle
. Note that, from
Taylor
’s formula, we obtain that

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}}_{x,y}^{K}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{n}{\binom{n}{k}\left\langle f,\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{k}\right\rangle}-\frac{y-x}{K}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\left\langle\partial_{i}f,\left[\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{n}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(y-x)^{2}}{2K^{2}}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum\limits_{j\,=\,1}^{n}{\left\langle\partial^{2}_{ij}f,\left[\mu-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{n}\right\rangle}}+O\left(\frac{\left|y-x\right|^{3}}{K^{3}}\right).

By abuse of notation, we do not indicate the orders of the products of measure
\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{k}
.

Step 1. Decomposition and study of
{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K}
.
Note that
{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K}=\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{5}{{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{i}^{K}}
where

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{1}^{K}

\displaystyle:=b(z,z)\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{n}{\binom{n}{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\left\langle f,\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{k}\right\rangle}}},

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{2}^{K}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{b(z,z)}{K}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum_{k\,=\,0}^{n}{\binom{n}{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)(y-x)\left\langle\partial_{i}f,\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{k}\right\rangle}}}},

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{3}^{K}

\displaystyle:=\frac{b(z,z)}{2K^{2}}\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum_{k\,=\,0}^{n}{\binom{n}{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)(y-x)^{2}\left\langle\partial_{ij}^{2}f,\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{k}\right\rangle}}}},

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{4}^{K}

\displaystyle:=O\left(\frac{M_{3}\left(\mu\right)}{K^{3}}\right),

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{5}^{K}

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)}\left[b\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}y+z\right)-b(z,z)\right]{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}}_{x,y}^{K}}.

We first study precisely the
{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{2}^{K}
term by explaining the case
k=0
,
k=1
and
k\geqslant 2
. We will just give the result for the other
{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{i}^{K}
,
i\in\{1,3,5\}
: the approach remains the same. By the centered assumption of
\mu
, note that for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)(y-x)\left\langle\partial_{i}f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle}}=0.

Then, using again the centered assumption of
\mu
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\binom{n}{1}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)(y-x)\left\langle\partial_{i}f,\mu^{n-1}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]\right\rangle}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum_{j\,=\,1}^{n}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)}}}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt\times\left[x\partial_{i}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{j-1},x,x_{j+1}\cdots,x_{n}\right)-x\partial_{i}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{j-1},y,x_{j+1}\cdots,x_{n}\right)\phantom{\frac{1}{K}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip-9.95863pt\left.\phantom{\frac{1}{K}}-y\partial_{i}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{j-1},x,x_{j+1}\cdots,x_{n}\right)+y\partial_{i}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{j-1},y,x_{j+1}\cdots,x_{n}\right)\right]\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\,=\,1\\
\ell\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{n}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x_{\ell})}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt=\frac{2}{K}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum_{j\,=\,1}^{n}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)x\partial_{i}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{j-1},x,x_{j+1},\cdots,x_{n}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\,=\,1\\
\ell\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{n}{\mu\left(\mathrm{d}x_{\ell}\right)}}}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt=\frac{2}{K}\left\langle\left(\nabla f\cdot\bm{1}\right)\left(\bullet\cdot\bm{1}\right),\mu^{n}\right\rangle.

As
\nabla f
is bounded, we deduce that for all
k\geqslant 2
, for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}y)(y-x)\left\langle\partial_{i}f,\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{y}}{K}\right]^{k}\right\rangle}}=O\left(\frac{M_{1}(\mu)}{K^{2}}\right).

Therefore, we deduce that

{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{2}^{K}=-\frac{2b(z,z)}{K^{2}}\left\langle\left(\nabla f\cdot\bm{1}\right)\left(\bullet\cdot\bm{1}\right),\mu^{n}\right\rangle+O\left(\frac{M_{1}(\mu)}{K^{3}}\right).

In similar way and using Assumptions
(A)
we have that

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{1}^{K}

\displaystyle=\frac{b(z,z)}{K^{2}}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\,=\,1\\
j\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left[\left\langle\Phi_{i,j}f,\mu^{n-1}\right\rangle-\left\langle f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle\right]}}+O\left(\frac{1}{K^{3}}\right),

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{3}^{K}

\displaystyle=\frac{b(z,z)}{K^{2}}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum_{j\,=\,1}^{n}{\left\langle K_{i,j}f,\mu^{n+1}\right\rangle}}+O\left(\frac{M_{2}\left(\mu\right)}{K^{3}}\right),

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}_{5}^{K}

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}}{K}\left[M_{1}\left(\mu\right)+M_{2}\left(\mu\right)\right]\right).

Step 2. Decomposition and study of
{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}^{K}
.
For all
\alpha,h\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we denote
\theta_{m}\left(\alpha,h\right):=\theta\left(\alpha\right)m\left(\alpha,h\right)
. Note that
{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}^{K}=\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{5}{{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{i}^{K}}
where

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{1}^{K}

\displaystyle:=\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{n}{\binom{n}{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\theta_{m}(z,h)\left\langle f,\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{x+\frac{h}{\sqrt{K}}}}{K}\right]^{k}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}h}}},

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{2}^{K}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{K^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum_{k\,=\,0}^{n}{\binom{n}{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\theta_{m}(z,h)h\left\langle\partial_{i}f,\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{x+\frac{h}{\sqrt{K}}}}{K}\right]^{k}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}h}}}},

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{3}^{K}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2K^{3}}\sum\limits_{i,j\,=\,1}^{n}{\sum_{k\,=\,0}^{n}{\binom{n}{k}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\theta_{m}(z,h)h^{2}\left\langle\partial_{ij}^{2}f,\mu^{n-k}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{x+\frac{h}{\sqrt{K}}}}{K}\right]^{k}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}h}}}},

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{4}^{K}

\displaystyle:=O\left(\frac{1}{K^{\frac{9}{2}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\theta_{m}(z,h)\left|h\right|^{3}\mathrm{d}h}}\right),

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{5}^{K}

\displaystyle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\left[\theta_{m}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}x+z,h\right)-\theta_{m}(z,h)\right]{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(C)}}}}}_{x,x+h/\sqrt{K}}^{K}}\,\mathrm{d}h}.

Note that, from
Taylor
’s formula and Assumptions
(A)
, we have that

\displaystyle\binom{n}{1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\theta_{m}(z,h)\left\langle f,\mu^{n-1}\left[-\frac{\delta_{x}}{K}+\frac{\delta_{x+\frac{h}{\sqrt{K}}}}{K}\right]\right\rangle\mathrm{d}h}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{n}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\mu(\mathrm{d}x_{i})\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\theta_{m}(z,h)\left[\frac{h}{\sqrt{K}}\partial_{i}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{i},\cdots,x_{n}\right)\right.}}}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.+\frac{h^{2}}{2K}\partial_{ii}^{2}f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{i},\cdots,x_{n}\right)+O\left(\frac{\left|h\right|^{3}}{K^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)\right]\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\,=\,1\\
\ell\,\neq\,i\end{subarray}}^{n}{\mu\left(\mathrm{d}x_{\ell}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=\frac{\theta(z)m_{2}(z)}{K^{2}}\left\langle\frac{\Delta f}{2},\mu^{n}\right\rangle+O\left(\frac{1}{K^{\frac{5}{2}}}\right).\hskip 244.69386pt

In similar way to Step 1, we obtain that

{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{1}^{K}=\frac{\theta(z)m_{2}(z)}{K^{2}}\left\langle\frac{\Delta f}{2},\mu^{n}\right\rangle+O\left(\frac{1}{K^{\frac{5}{2}}}\right),\qquad{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{2}^{K}=O\left(\frac{1}{K^{3}}\right),

{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{3}^{K}=O\left(\frac{1}{K^{3}}\right),\quad\ {\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{4}^{K}=O\left(\frac{1}{K^{\frac{9}{2}}}\right)\quad\ {\rm{and}}\quad\ {\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(B)}}}}}_{5}^{K}=O\left(\frac{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}}{K^{\frac{3}{2}}}M_{1}\left(\mu\right)\right).

and using Assumptions (
5
) the announced result follows.

Step 3. Martingality.
By classical arguments, since
M^{K,P_{f,n}}
is bounded, we obtain that
M^{K,P_{f,n}}
is a square integrable martingale started at
0
.

4
Moments estimates

Lemmas
28
and
3.7
lead us to look for moment estimates. In Lemma
28
we have given the
Doob
semi-martingale decomposition of the second-order moment and note that involves the third-order moment in the error term. The presence of the higher order moment generates a difficulty to obtain a fine control of the second order moment. This difficulty is overcome by introducing the stopping time
\check{\tau}^{K}
, given by (
15
). In Section
4.1
, we give estimates of the moment of order
6
and some corollaries useful for Sections
5
and
6
. In Section
4.2
we establish estimates in expectation and probability of the second order moment up to the stopping time
\check{\tau}^{K}
and we also control the martingale bracket of the
Doob
decomposition given in Lemma
28
. In Sections
4.3
and
4.4
, we prove that the stopping time
\tau^{K}:=\check{\tau}^{K}\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K}
, given by (
13
), converges in probability to
+\infty
when
K\to+\infty
using coupling arguments between
M_{2}\left(\mu^{K}\right)
and a biased random walk on
{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, reflected in
1
, and large deviations estimates for this random walk.

4.1
Estimates of the moment of order
6

In this section, we establish a technical lemma useful for Lemma
4.2
below. The idea is to obtain an explicit upper bound of the moments of order
6
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Given any continuous function
y(t)
satisfying

y(t)\leqslant y(s)+\int_{s}^{t}{F\left(y(r)\right)\mathrm{d}r},\qquad\forall s\leqslant t

(29)

for some
Lipschitz
function
F:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, then
y(s)\leqslant z(s)
for all
s\geqslant 0
, where
z(t)
is the unique solution of

z(t)=z(s)+\int_{s}^{t}{F\left(z(r)\right)\mathrm{d}r},\qquad\forall s\leqslant t

(30)

such that
z(0)=y(0)
.

Proof.

First, it is sufficient to prove that, for all
\varepsilon>0
,
y(s)\leqslant z_{\varepsilon}(s)
for all
s\geqslant 0
, where
z_{\varepsilon}
is the unique solution of

z_{\varepsilon}(t)=z_{\varepsilon}(s)+\int_{s}^{t}{\left[F\left(z_{\varepsilon}(r)\right)+\varepsilon\right]\mathrm{d}r},\qquad\forall s\leqslant t

(31)

such that
z_{\varepsilon}(0)=y(0)+\varepsilon
. Indeed, it follows from (
30
) and (
31
) that, for all
t\geqslant 0
and
\varepsilon>0

\left|z(t)-z_{\varepsilon}(t)\right|\leqslant\varepsilon(1+t)+C_{\rm Lip}\int_{0}^{t}{\left|z(s)-z_{\varepsilon}(s)\right|\mathrm{d}s}

where
C_{\rm Lip}
if the
Lipschitz
constant of
F
.
Gronwall
’s lemma then entails

\left|z(t)-z_{\varepsilon}(t)\right|\leqslant\varepsilon(1+t)\exp\left(C_{\rm Lip}t\right)\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\to 0]{}0

so that
y(t)\leqslant z_{\varepsilon}(t)
, for all
\varepsilon>0
indeed implies
y(t)\leqslant z(t)
.

So, let
\varepsilon>0
be fixed and let us prove that
y(t)\leqslant z_{\varepsilon}(t)
for all
t\geqslant 0
. Define

\vartheta_{\varepsilon}:=\inf\left\{t\geqslant 0\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1}}}\hskip-15.6491pt\right.y(t)\geqslant z_{\varepsilon}(t)\right\}.

Our goal is to prove that
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}=+\infty
.

By continuity of
y
,
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}>0
and, if
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}<\infty
,
y\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\right)=z_{\varepsilon}\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\right)
. Assume by contradiction that
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}<\infty
. Then, using (
29
) and (
31
), for all
\delta\in\left(0,\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\right)
,

\frac{z_{\varepsilon}\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\right)-y\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\right)-\left(z_{\varepsilon}\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}-\delta\right)-y\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}-\delta\right)\right)}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}}\geqslant\varepsilon+\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}}-\delta}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vartheta_{\varepsilon}}}{\left[F\left(z_{\varepsilon}(r)\right)-F(y(r))\right]\mathrm{d}r}.

By continuity of
z_{\varepsilon}
and
y
, the last term of the right-hand side converges to
0
when
\delta\to 0
. Hence the left-hand side is positive for all
\delta
small enough. Since
z_{\varepsilon}\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\right)=y\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}\right)
, this implies that

z_{\varepsilon}\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}-\delta\right)-y\left(\vartheta_{\varepsilon}-\delta\right)<0

for such
\delta
. This contradicts the definition of
\vartheta_{\varepsilon}
as the first time
t
such that
z_{\varepsilon}(t)\leqslant y(t)
. ∎

Lemma 4.2
.

There exists
K_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
large enough and two constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
and
t\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(M_{6}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)\leqslant C_{1}\exp\left(-\frac{9\underline{b}}{28K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t\right)+C_{2}

Proof.

Step 1. Pathwise inequality.
Note that for all
t\geqslant 0
,

M_{7}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)\leqslant{\rm{Diam}}\left({\rm{Supp\,}}{\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}}\right)M_{6}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)\leqslant\frac{M_{6}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2}}}.

(32)

Introducing for all
t\geqslant 0
,
A_{t}:=\frac{3}{4}M_{6}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)+7M_{4}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)-4\widetilde{M}_{3}^{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)+\frac{3}{2}M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)
, we obtain from Lemma
3.7
, (
32
) and Assumptions
(A)
that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
t>s\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle A_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}

\displaystyle\leqslant A_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}-\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left\{\left(\underline{b}-\frac{C}{K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\right)\left[3M_{6}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)+\frac{9}{2}M_{4}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)+6M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)\right]\right.}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt-\left.\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}\left[\frac{91}{4}M_{4}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)+42M_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}r\right\}+{\rm Mart}_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}-{\rm Mart}_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}

where
\left({\rm Mart}_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is a martingale. Note that, choosing
K_{0}
large enough, for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
,
\underline{b}-CK^{-\varepsilon/2}>\underline{b}/2
.
From the inequalities
M_{2}^{2}(\mu)\leqslant M_{4}(\mu)
and
M_{4}(\mu)\leqslant M_{6}^{2/3}\left(\mu\right)\leqslant\alpha M_{6}(\mu)+1/\alpha^{2}
with
\alpha:=2\underline{b}/259\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}
, we deduce that

\frac{3\underline{b}}{2}M_{6}\left(\mu\right)-\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}\left[\frac{91}{4}M_{4}\left(\mu\right)+42M_{2}^{2}\left(\mu\right)\right]\geqslant\frac{3\underline{b}}{2}M_{6}\left(\mu\right)-\frac{259\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{4}M_{4}(\mu)\geqslant\underline{b}M_{6}\left(\mu\right)-\frac{259^{3}\overline{\theta}^{3}\overline{m}_{2}^{3}}{16\underline{b}^{2}}.

Thus, for all
t>s\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle A_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}

\displaystyle\leqslant A_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}+{\rm Mart}_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}-{\rm Mart}_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-\frac{\underline{b}}{2K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left[2M_{6}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)+\frac{9}{2}M_{4}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)+6M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)-\left(\frac{259\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{2\underline{b}}\right)^{3}\right]\mathrm{d}r}

\displaystyle\leqslant A_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}-\frac{9\underline{b}}{28K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left(A_{r}-\frac{14}{9}\left(\frac{259\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{2\underline{b}}\right)^{3}\right)\mathrm{d}r}+{\rm Mart}_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}-{\rm Mart}_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}

Step 2. Conclusion.
Let us consider the stochastic process
\left(N\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
defined by

N\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}A_{t}&{\rm if}&s<t\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\\
\left(A_{\check{\tau}^{K}}-C_{0}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{3\underline{b}}{4K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\left(t-\check{\tau}^{K}\right)\right)+C_{0}&{\rm if}&s<\check{\tau}^{K}<t\\
0&{\rm if}&\check{\tau}^{K}<s<t\end{array}\right.

where
C_{0}:=\frac{14}{9}\left(\frac{259\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{2\underline{b}}\right)^{3}
. From Step 1, for all
t>s\geqslant 0
,

N\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\leqslant N\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)-\frac{9\underline{b}}{28K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{s}^{t}{\left[N\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)-C_{0}\right]\mathrm{d}r}+{\rm Mart}_{t}-{\rm Mart}_{s}

which entails

y(t)\leqslant y(s)-\frac{9\underline{b}}{28K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{s}^{t}{\left(y(r)-C_{0}\right)\mathrm{d}r}

where
y(t):={\mathbb{E}}\left(N\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\right)
. From Lemma
4.1
, we deduce that for all
t\geqslant 0
,
y(t)\leqslant z(t)
where
z(t)
is the unique solution of

z(t)=z(s)-\frac{9\underline{b}}{28K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{s}^{t}{\left(z(r)-C_{0}\right)\mathrm{d}r}

such that
z(0)=A_{0}
, i.e.
z(t)=(z(0)-C_{0})\exp\left(-\frac{9\underline{b}}{28K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t\right)+C_{0}
.
From (
7
) then (
4
), note that

M_{6}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)=\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{6},\mu_{0}^{K}\right\rangle=\frac{1}{K^{3}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{6}}}\left\langle\left({\rm{id}}-x_{0}\right)^{6},\nu_{0}^{K}\right\rangle\leqslant C_{6}^{\star}.

From
Cauchy-Schwarz
’s inequality
\widetilde{M}_{3}^{2}(\mu)\leqslant M_{4}(\mu)M_{2}(\mu)
, note that
z(0)\leqslant 21C_{6}^{\star}/4
and so, for all
t\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(M_{6}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4}{3}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left[\frac{3}{4}M_{6}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)+3M_{4}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)+\frac{3}{2}M_{2}^{3}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\right]{\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4}{3}z(t)\leqslant\frac{4}{3}\left(\frac{21}{4}\left(C_{6}^{\star}-C_{0}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{9\underline{b}}{28K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t\right)+C_{0}\right)

and the announced result follows. ∎

The following lemma will be useful in the proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Lemma 4.3
.

For all
t\geqslant 0
,

\sup_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\mathrm{d}s}\right)}<\infty.

Proof.

Let
t\geqslant 0
be fixed. By
Fubini
’s theorem and Lemma
4.2
, there exists two constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
such that

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}{M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}\mathrm{d}s}\right)\leqslant C_{1}\int_{0}^{t}{\exp\left(-\frac{9\underline{b}}{28K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}s\right)\mathrm{d}s}+C_{2}t\leqslant(C_{1}+C_{2})t

which ends the proof. ∎

The last lemma has the following consequence, used in Sections
5
and
6
.

Corollary 4.4
.

For all
T>0
, the family

\left(\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{M_{5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\mathrm{d}s}\right)_{t\in[0,T],K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}

is uniformly integrable.

Proof.

Note that for all
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, for all
t\in[0,T]
, for all
A\in(0,+\infty)
,

{\mathds{1}}_{\int_{0}^{t}{M_{5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}}\mathrm{d}s\geqslant A}\int_{0}^{t}{M_{5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\mathrm{d}s}\leqslant\frac{1}{\sqrt[5]{A}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}{M_{5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\mathrm{d}s}\right)^{6/5},

we deduce from
Hölder
’inequality that, for all
t\in[0,T]
, for all
A\in(0,+\infty)
,

\displaystyle\sup_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{E}}\left({\mathds{1}}_{\int_{0}^{t}{M_{5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}}\mathrm{d}s\geqslant A}\int_{0}^{t}{M_{5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\mathrm{d}s}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\leqslant\frac{T}{\sqrt[5]{A}}\sup_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}{M_{5}^{6/5}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\mathrm{d}s}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\leqslant\frac{T}{\sqrt[5]{A}}\sup_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}{M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\mathrm{d}s}\right)}.

From Lemma
4.3
, we deduce that the right hand side of the previous inequality goes to
0
when
A\to+\infty
and so the announced result. ∎

4.2
Some inequalities on the moment of order
2

Lemma 4.5
.

There exists
K_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
large enough such that for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
, the process
M_{2}\left(\mu^{K}\right)
satisfies for all
t\geqslant 0
the following inequalities​​ :

\displaystyle M_{2}\left(\mu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}}^{K}\right)-\frac{5\overline{b}}{2K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left[M_{2}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)-\frac{2\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}}{5\overline{b}}+\frac{2\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{5\overline{b}K}\right]\mathrm{d}r}+M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-M_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}}

(33)

\displaystyle\leqslant M_{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)\leqslant M_{2}\left(\mu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}}^{K}\right)-\frac{\underline{b}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left[M_{2}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)-\frac{\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{\underline{b}}\right]\mathrm{d}r}+M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-M_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}}

where
M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}
is defined in
Proposition
3.5
. Moreover, for
t\geqslant 0
,

{\mathbb{E}}\left(M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)\leqslant{\mathbb{E}}\left(M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)-\frac{\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{\underline{b}}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\underline{b}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t\right)+\frac{\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{\underline{b}}.

Proof.

Note that for all
t\geqslant 0
,

M_{3}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)\leqslant{\rm{Diam}}\left({\rm{Supp\,}}{\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}}\right)M_{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)\leqslant\frac{M_{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2}}}.

(34)

From Lemma
28
, (
34
) and Assumptions
(A)
, there exists two constants
C,\widetilde{C}>0
such that for all
t>s\geqslant 0

\displaystyle M_{2}\left(\mu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}}^{K}\right)-\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left(\left[2\overline{b}+\frac{C}{K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\right]M_{2}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)-\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}+\frac{\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}}{K}\right)\mathrm{d}r}+M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-M_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leqslant M_{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 4.26773pt\leqslant M_{2}\left(\mu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}}^{K}\right)-\frac{1}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left(\left[2\underline{b}-\frac{\widetilde{C}}{K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}\right]M_{2}\left(\mu_{r}^{K}\right)-\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}+\frac{\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}}{K}\right)\mathrm{d}r}+M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-M_{s\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}}.

Noting that for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
,
2\underline{b}-\widetilde{C}K^{-\varepsilon/2}>\underline{b}
and
2\overline{b}+CK^{-\varepsilon/2}<5\overline{b}/2
, the announced first result follows. In similar way to Step 2 of the proof of Lemma
4.2
, we obtain the second part of the announced result. ∎

Corollary 4.6
.

For all
t\geqslant 0
,
{\mathbb{E}}\left(M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)\leqslant\max\left\{C_{2}^{\star},\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}/\underline{b}}\right\}
.

Proof.

From (
7
) and (
4
), note that

M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)=\left\langle{\rm{id}}^{2},\mu_{0}^{K}\right\rangle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leqslant C_{2}^{\star}}.

The announced result follows from Lemma
4.5
. ∎

The goal of the following result is to bound the martingale bracket
\left\langle M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right\rangle_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}
. To do this, we exploit the martingale approximation of the moment of order 6 in order to control the dominant term
M_{4}\left(\mu^{K}_{s}\right)-M_{2}^{2}\left(\mu^{K}_{s}\right)
in (
28
).

Lemma 4.7
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, for all
t\geqslant 0
the martingale bracket
\left\langle M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right\rangle_{t}
satisfies

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right\rangle_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)\leqslant\frac{C}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left(t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}\right).

Proof.

From Lemma
28
and
Hölder
’s inequalities
M_{2}^{2}(\mu)\leqslant M_{4}(\mu)\leqslant M_{6}^{2/3}\leqslant 1+M_{6}(\mu)
and
M_{5}\left(\mu\right)\leqslant M_{6}\left(\mu\right)^{5/6}\leqslant 1+M_{6}\left(\mu\right)
, we deduce that there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
t\geqslant 0

\displaystyle\left\langle M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right\rangle_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{\left(1+M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{K}\right)\mathrm{d}s}.

The announced result follows from
Fubini
’s theorem and Lemma
4.2
. ∎

4.3
Convergence to
+\infty
of the stopping time
\tau^{K}
and support concentration property

The main result of this section is the following convergence result for the stopping time
\tau^{K}
, defined by (
13
), when
K\to+\infty
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Under
Assumption (
5
)
,
\tau^{K}
converges in probability to
+\infty
when
K\to+\infty
.

The proof is based on the next lemma proved in Section
4.4
below. Thanks to Proposition
4.8
, we deduce the support concentration property of
\left(\nu_{t/K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}^{K}\right)
given by (
6
). Indeed, with probability which tends to
1
when
K\to+\infty
,
{\rm{Diam}}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\leqslant\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2}}}
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Hence,

{\rm{Diam}}\left(\nu_{t/K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}^{K}\right)={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}{\rm{Diam}}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\leqslant\frac{1}{K^{1+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}

and (
6
) follows.

Lemma 4.9
.

Under
Assumption (
5
)
, for all
T\geqslant 0
,

\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\wedge T\right)}=\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)}\geqslant K^{\varepsilon}\right)}=0.

Proof of Proposition
4.8
.

Note that for all
T\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\check{\tau}^{K}<T\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K}\right)\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists t<T\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K},{\rm{Diam}}\left({\rm{Supp\,}}\mu_{t}^{K}\right)>\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2}}}\right).

As for all
t\geqslant 0
, for all
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
{\rm{Diam}}\left({\rm{Supp\,}}\mu_{t}^{K}\right)^{2}\leqslant\left(2\max{\left\{\left|x\right|\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right.x\in{\rm{Supp\,}}\mu_{t}^{K}\right\}}\right)^{2}\leqslant 4KM_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists t<T\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K},{\rm{Diam}}\left({\rm{Supp\,}}\mu_{t}^{K}\right)>\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists t<T\wedge,\widehat{\tau}^{K}M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)>\frac{1}{4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}K^{4+\varepsilon}}\right).

According to (
5
), we deduce that the following inequality
4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}K^{4+\varepsilon}\leqslant 1/K^{\varepsilon}
holds true when
K\to+\infty
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\to 0
. Hence, we deduce that

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists t<T\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K},M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)>\frac{1}{4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}K^{4+\varepsilon}}\right)\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\exists t<T\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K},M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)>K^{\varepsilon}\right)=0.

Now, for all
T>0
,
\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}\geqslant T\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K}\right\}=\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}\geqslant\widehat{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cup\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}\geqslant T\right\}
, and so

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}\geqslant T\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\tau^{K}<T\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left[\left\{\widehat{\tau}^{K}<T\right\}\cap\left\{\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right]\cup\left\{\widehat{\tau}^{K}<T\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant T\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}\right).

Note that for all
T>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tau^{K}<T\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\check{\tau}^{K}<T\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant T\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}\right).

Hence, from Lemma
4.9
, we have for all
T\geqslant 0
,
\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tau^{K}<T\right)}=0
and the announced result follows. ∎

4.4
Proof of Lemma
4.9

Note that if
M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)
becomes larger than
\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}/\underline{b}
, the drift term in the right-hand side of (
33
) is then negative preventing
M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K}\right)
to become excessively large unless the martingale
M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}
has an exceptional path with large increments on a small time interval. We then expect to have large deviations estimates and bounds on the time of exit from attractive domains for
M_{2}\left(\mu^{K}\right)
. However, we cannot consider establishing directly a large deviation principle on
M_{2}\left(\mu^{K}\right)
, in particular because of the slow-fast limit. The approach considered below is based on coupling arguments between
M_{2}\left(\mu^{K}\right)
and a simpler process for which large deviations estimates are known.

Let us introduce
u_{0}:=0
and for all
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
the real number
u_{\ell}:=3^{\ell}K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}
and the interval
{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}:=\left[u_{\ell-1},u_{\ell+1}\right)
. We will look at the process
M_{2}\left(\mu^{K}\right)
at successive exit times of
\left({\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}\right)_{\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
. We set
L_{0}^{K}:=1
when
M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\in[0,2u_{1}+1]
and
L_{0}^{K}:=\ell_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
when
M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\in\left(2u_{\ell_{0}-1}+1,2u_{\ell_{0}}+1\right]
. We also set
T_{0}^{K}:=0
and by induction on
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, if
\left(L_{i}^{K}\right)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k}
and
\left(T_{i}^{K}\right)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k}
are constructed,

T_{k+1}^{K}:=\inf\left\{t\geqslant T_{k}^{K}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\notin{\mathcal{I}}_{L_{k}^{K}}\right\}

and

L_{k+1}^{K}:=\min\left\{\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.M_{2}\left(\mu_{T_{k+1}^{K}}^{K}\right)\leqslant 2u_{\ell}+1\right\}.

Note that from (
4
), for
K
large enough,
L_{0}^{K}=1
because
M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\leqslant C_{2}^{\star}
. Since
\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is a pure jump process, note that
\left(M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is also a pure jump process. The definition of
u_{\ell}
is motivated by the fact that, for all
k\geqslant 0
,
L_{k+1}^{K}\leqslant L_{k}^{K}+1
, which follows from the next lemma.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
\vartheta_{1}
be the first jump time of
\mu^{K}
. Then, for
K
large enough

M_{2}\left(\mu_{\vartheta_{1}}^{K}\right)\leqslant 2M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)+1.

In particular, for all
t>0
,
\Delta M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right):=M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)-M_{2}\left(\mu_{t^{-}}^{K}\right)\leqslant M_{2}\left(\mu_{t^{-}}^{K}\right)+1
.

Proof.

Let us consider
\mu_{0}^{K}:=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\,=\,1}^{K}{\delta_{x_{k}}}
and
y=\left(y_{k}\right)_{1\leqslant k\leqslant K}
defined by

y=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{i-1},x_{i}+\frac{H}{\sqrt{K}},x_{i+1},\cdots,x_{K}\right)&{\rm on}\ E_{1},\\
\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{j-1},x_{i},x_{j+1},\cdots,x_{K}\right)&{\rm on}\ E_{2},\end{array}\right.

where
H
follows the mutation law
m(x_{i},\mathrm{d}h)
,
E_{1}
is the event “a mutation occurs at time
\vartheta_{1}
” and
E_{2}
is the event “a resampling between
i
and
j
occurs at time
\vartheta_{1}
”. Note that
\mu_{\vartheta_{1}}^{K}=\tau_{-\left\langle{\rm{id}},\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\,=\,1}^{K}{\delta_{y_{k}}}\right\rangle}\sharp\left(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k\,=\,1}^{K}{\delta_{y_{k}}}\right)
. Hence, noting that for all
k\in\{1,\cdots,K\}
,

\left|x_{k}\right|\leqslant\sqrt{K}\left(1+M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\right)

and using Assumption
(A2)
, for
K
large enough we have that

\displaystyle M_{2}\left(\mu_{\vartheta_{1}}^{K}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant M_{2}\left(\frac{1}{K}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{K}{\delta_{y_{i}}}\right)=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{K}{y_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\cfrac{1}{K}{\displaystyle{\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{K}{x_{k}^{2}}}}+\cfrac{2A_{m}x_{k}}{K^{\frac{3}{2}}}+\cfrac{A_{m}^{2}}{K^{2}}&\quad{\rm{on}}\quad\ E_{1},\\
\cfrac{1}{K}{\displaystyle{\sum_{k\,=\,1}^{K}{x_{k}^{2}}}}+\cfrac{x_{j}^{2}-x_{i}^{2}}{K}&\quad{\rm{on}}\quad\ E_{2},\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\leqslant\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)+\cfrac{2A_{m}\left(1+M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\right)}{K}+\cfrac{A_{m}^{2}}{K^{2}}&\quad{\rm{on}}\quad E_{1},\\
2M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)&\quad{\rm{on}}\quad E_{2},\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\leqslant 2M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)+1.

∎

Each of the previous steps will be called
transitions of
M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)
. The idea of the proof of Lemma
4.9
is to estimate the transition probabilities of the sequence
\left(L_{k}^{K}\right)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in order to construct a coupling between
\left(L_{k}^{K}\right)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and a biased random walk on
{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
reflected in
1
.

At time
\widehat{\tau}^{K}
,
M_{2}\left(\mu_{\widehat{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor}
where
\lfloor x\rfloor
is the lower integer part of
x
. So, the number of transitions of
M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)
before
\widehat{\tau}^{K}
is greater than or equal to
T_{k_{0}}^{K}
where
k_{0}
is the first integer such that
L_{k_{0}}^{K}=\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor
.

By using estimates on the number of steps that a biased random walk takes to reach
\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor
and estimates on the durations between two transitions
T_{k+1}^{K}-T_{k}^{K}
, we will deduce a lower bound, exponential in
K
, on
\widehat{\tau}^{K}
. An additional difficulty comes from the fact that the previous coupling argument is only valid up to time
\check{\tau}^{K}
. Hence we will construct a coupling that takes into account the possibility that
\check{\tau}^{K}
happens during each transition step. The proof is divided into four steps: in Section
4.4.1
, we characterise the behaviour of the first transition step; in Section
4.4.2
, the proposed coupling is constructed; in Section
4.4.3
, we give estimates on the first exit time from an attractive domain for random walks; finally, we conclude in Section
4.4.4
.

4.4.1
One-step transitions

In this section we look at only one transition: we suppose that
M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\in\left[2u_{\ell-1}+1,2u_{\ell}+1\right)
for
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
fixed and we look for bounds on the first transition probabilities of
M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)
and on time
T_{1}^{K}
. The main result is the following.

Lemma 4.11
.

For all
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
, for all
\mu_{0}^{K}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1,K}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
such that

M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\in\left[2u_{\ell-1}+1,2u_{\ell}+1\right),

there exist a constant
C>0
,
K_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
such that for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left[\left\{L_{1}^{K}=\ell-1\right\}\cap\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}>T_{1}^{K}\geqslant K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\right\}\right]\cup\left\{T_{1}^{K}\geqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right)\geqslant\frac{1}{2}+\eta_{K},

(35)

where
\eta_{K}:=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{C}{K^{\varepsilon/2}}
.

The proof of the previous result is based on Corollary
4.12
which is obtained as a straightforward consequence of Lemma
4.5
.

Corollary 4.12
.

Let
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
be fixed. There exists
K_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
large enough such that for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
, for all
\mu_{0}^{K}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1,K}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
such that
M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\in\left[2u_{\ell-1}+1,2u_{\ell}+1\right)
, we have for all
t\leqslant T_{1}^{K}
that

\displaystyle 2u_{\ell-1}+1-\frac{5\overline{b}u_{\ell+1}+\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}}{2K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}+M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\leqslant M_{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)\leqslant 2u_{\ell}+1-\frac{\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}+M_{t\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}.

Proof of Lemma
4.11
.

By passing on the complementary of (
35
), it is equivalent to prove that

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left[\left\{L_{1}^{K}=\ell+1\right\}\cup\left\{T_{1}^{K}<K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\right\}\right]\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right)<\frac{1}{2}-\eta_{K}.

As for all events
A,B
,
{\mathbb{P}}\left(A\cup B\right)={\mathbb{P}}\left(A\cap B^{c}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}(B)
, we have

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left[\left\{L_{1}^{K}=\ell+1\right\}\cup\left\{T_{1}^{K}<K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\right\}\right]\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right)

(36)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{L_{1}^{K}=\ell+1\right\}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K}\geqslant K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K}<K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{L_{1}^{K}=\ell+1\right\}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K}<K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\right\}\right).

Step 1. Control of the first right-hand term of (
36
).
Let us consider the martingale
\left({\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
defined by

{\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}:=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}M_{t}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}&{\rm{if}}&t\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\\
M_{\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}+\frac{1}{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}}B_{t-\check{\tau}^{K}}&{\rm{if}}&t>\check{\tau}^{K}\end{array}\right.

where
\left(B_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is a standard Brownian motion independent of
\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
. Note that from Lemma
4.7
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
t\geqslant 0
we have

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{t}\right)\leqslant\frac{C}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t.

(37)

Let us consider the process
\left(M_{2}^{K,+}(t)\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
defined by

M_{2}^{K,+}(t):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)&{\rm if}&t\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\\
M_{2}\left(\mu_{\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K}\right)-\frac{\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}\left(t-\check{\tau}^{K}\right)+\left({\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}-{\rm Mart}_{\check{\tau}^{K}}^{K,+}\right)&{\rm{if}}&t>\check{\tau}^{K}\end{array}\right..

Note that from Corollary
4.12
, for all
t\geqslant 0
,

2u_{\ell-1}+1-\frac{5\overline{b}u_{\ell+1}+\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}}{2K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t+{\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}\leqslant M_{2}^{K,+}(t)\leqslant 2u_{\ell}+1-\frac{\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t+{\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}.

(38)

By
Girsanov
’s theorem, the process
\left(E_{t}^{K,+}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
defined for all
t\geqslant 0
by

E_{t}^{K,+}:=\exp\left({\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}-\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{t}\right)

is a local martingale. Let
t_{0}^{K}:=\frac{6K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}{\underline{b}}
be fixed and let us consider

\displaystyle T_{1}^{K,+}

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{t\geqslant 0\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.M_{2}^{K,+}\left(t\right)\not\in{\mathcal{I}}_{\ell}\right\},

\displaystyle L_{1}^{K,+}

\displaystyle:=\min\left\{\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.M_{2}^{K,+}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}\right)\leqslant 2u_{\ell}-1\right\}.

From (
38
) and denoting
\Gamma:=\left\{\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}}\leqslant\frac{2\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}\right\}
, we have

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(E_{T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}}^{K,+}\right)\geqslant{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(E_{T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}}^{K,+}{\mathds{1}}_{\Gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right){\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\exp\left(M_{2}^{K,+}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}\right)+\frac{\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt-\left.\left.\frac{1}{2}\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\Gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right){\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\exp\left(M_{2}^{K,+}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}\right)\right){\mathds{1}}_{\Gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)\left[\exp\left(u_{\ell+1}\right){\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{L_{1}^{K,+}=\ell+1\right\}\cap\Gamma\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}\leqslant t_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\left.{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{L_{1}^{K,+}\leqslant\ell-1\right\}\cap\Gamma\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}\leqslant t_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)\right].

As for all events
A,B,C
,
{\mathbb{P}}\left(A\cap B\cap C\right)\geqslant{\mathbb{P}}(A)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(A\cap B^{c}\right)-{\mathbb{P}}\left(A\cap C^{c}\right)
, and denoting
q:={\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(L_{1}^{K,+}=\ell+1\right)
we have that

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\geqslant\exp\left(u_{\ell}-1\right)\left[q-{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{L_{1}^{K,+}=\ell+1\right\}\cap\Gamma^{c}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\left.{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{L_{1}^{K,+}=\ell+1\right\}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}>t_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)\left[1-q-{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{L_{1}^{K,+}\leqslant\ell-1\right\}\cap\Gamma^{c}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\left.-{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{L_{1}^{K,+}\leqslant\ell-1\right\}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}>t_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geqslant\left(\exp\left(u_{\ell}-1\right)-\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)\right)q+\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.33955pt-\left(\exp\left(u_{\ell}-1\right)+\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)\right)\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\Gamma^{c}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}>t_{0}^{K}\right)\right].

Then, considering
s_{0}^{K}:=\frac{u_{\ell-1}K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}{5\overline{b}u_{\ell+1}+\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}}<t_{0}^{K}
be fixed, we deduce that

\displaystyle q

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1-\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)}{\exp\left(u_{\ell}-1\right)-\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{\exp\left(u_{\ell}\right)+\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}\right)}{\exp\left(u_{\ell}\right)-\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}\right)}\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\Gamma^{c}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}>t_{0}^{K}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\exp\left(-u_{\ell}+1\right)\times\frac{1-\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)}{1-\exp\left(-u_{\ell+1}\right)}+\frac{\exp\left(u_{\ell}\right)+\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}\right)}{\exp\left(u_{\ell}\right)-\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\Gamma^{c}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}\geqslant s_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}>t_{0}^{K}\right)\right].

Denoting
\lceil a\rceil
the upper integer part of
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain on the one hand that there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\Gamma^{c}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}\geqslant s_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt={\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}}>\frac{2\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}\geqslant s_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt=\hskip-5.69046pt\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{\left\lceil\frac{t_{0}^{K}}{s_{0}^{K}}\right\rceil-1}{\hskip-8.5359pt{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}}>\frac{2\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{ks_{0}^{K}\leqslant T_{1}^{K,+}\leqslant(k+1)s_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}}>\frac{2\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}T_{1}^{K,+}\wedge t_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}\geqslant\lceil t_{0}^{K}/s_{0}^{K}\rceil s_{0}^{K}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\leqslant\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{\left\lceil\frac{t_{0}^{K}}{s_{0}^{K}}\right\rceil-1}{\hskip-8.5359pt{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{(k+1)s_{0}^{K}}>\frac{2\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}ks_{0}^{K}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{t_{0}^{K}}>\frac{2\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t_{0}^{K}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\leqslant K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\left(\sum\limits_{k\,=\,1}^{\lceil t_{0}^{K}/s_{0}^{K}\rceil-1}{\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{(k+1)s_{0}^{K}}\right)}{2\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}ks_{0}^{K}}+\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{t_{0}^{K}}\right)}{2\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}t_{0}^{K}}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\leqslant\frac{C_{1}}{K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}

where we use
Markov
’s inequality in the second inequality and (
38
) in the last inequality. On the other hand, we control
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right)
as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{M_{2}^{K,+}(t)}\geqslant u_{\ell+1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\inf_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{M_{2}^{K,+}(t)}<u_{\ell-1}\right\}\right).

Let us consider
K_{1}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
independent of
\ell
satisfying
K_{1}^{\varepsilon/2}\geqslant 2
. There exists a constant
C_{1}>0
such that for all
K\geqslant K_{1}
, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{M_{2}^{K,+}(t)}\geqslant u_{\ell+1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\overset{}{\leqslant}{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{\left\{2u_{\ell}+1-\frac{\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t+{\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}\right\}\geqslant u_{\ell+1}}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{{\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}}\geqslant u_{\ell}-1\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{\left|{\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}\right|}\geqslant\frac{3^{\ell}K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leqslant 4{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}}\left(\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{s_{0}^{K}}\right)\times\frac{4}{3^{2\ell}K^{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leqslant\frac{C_{1}}{K^{\varepsilon}}

where we use (
38
) in the first inequality,
Doob
’s maximal inequality in the fourth inequality and (
37
) in the last inequality. Now, let us consider
K_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
independent of
\ell
satisfying
K_{2}^{\varepsilon/2}\geqslant 4
. Using again (
38
),
Doob
’s maximal inequality, (
37
), and the definition of
s_{0}^{K}
, we deduce that there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
such that for all
K\geqslant K_{2}
, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\inf_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{M_{2}^{K,+}(t)}<u_{\ell-1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\inf_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{\left\{2u_{\ell-1}-1-\frac{5\overline{b}u_{\ell+1}+\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}}{2K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t+{\rm Mart}^{K,+}_{t}\right\}}<u_{\ell-1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\inf_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{{\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}}<-\frac{u_{\ell-1}}{2}+1\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant s_{0}^{K}}{\left|{\rm Mart}_{t}^{K,+}\right|}>\frac{u_{\ell-1}}{2}-1\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\leqslant\frac{C_{2}}{K^{\varepsilon}}.

Hence, for all
K\geqslant\max\left\{K_{1},K_{2}\right\}
,
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}<s_{0}^{K}\right)\leqslant\frac{C_{1}+C_{2}}{K^{\varepsilon}}
. Finally, from (
38
) and
Markov
’s inequality and the definition of
t_{0}^{K}
, note that there exists a constant
C_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(T_{1}^{K,+}>t_{0}^{K}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant t_{0}^{K}}{M_{2}^{K,+}(t)}<u_{\ell+1}\right\}\cap\left\{\inf_{0\leqslant t\leqslant t_{0}^{K}}{M_{2}^{K,+}(t)}\geqslant u_{\ell-1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left({\rm Mart}_{t_{0}^{K}}^{K,+}\geqslant u_{\ell-1}-2u_{\ell}-1+\frac{\underline{b}u_{\ell-1}}{K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}t_{0}^{K}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\langle{\rm Mart}^{K,+}\right\rangle_{t_{0}^{K}}\right)}{\left(u_{\ell-1}-1\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{3}}{K^{\varepsilon}}.

Hence, for all
K\geqslant\max{\left\{K_{1},K_{2}\right\}}
, we deduce that
q\leqslant\varepsilon_{K}
where

\varepsilon_{K}:=\exp\left(-u_{\ell}+1\right)\times\frac{1-\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}-1\right)}{1-\exp\left(-u_{\ell+1}\right)}+\frac{\exp\left(u_{\ell}\right)+\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}\right)}{\exp\left(u_{\ell}\right)-\exp\left(-2u_{\ell}\right)}\times\frac{C_{1}}{K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}}

and thus, for all
K\geqslant\max{\left\{K_{1},K_{2}\right\}}
,

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{L_{1}^{K}=\ell+1\right\}\right)={\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K,+}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{L_{1}^{K}=\ell+1\right\}\right)\leqslant q\leqslant\varepsilon_{K}.

Step 2. Control of the second right-hand term of (
36
).
Similarly, let us consider
K_{3}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
independent of
\ell
satisfying
K_{3}^{\varepsilon/2}\geqslant 2
. Using Corollary
4.12
,
Doob
’s maximal inequality and Lemma
4.7
in similar way to Step 1, there exists a constant
C_{4}>0
such that for all
K\geqslant K_{3}
we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)}\geqslant u_{\ell+1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{16{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\langle M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right\rangle_{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)}{3^{2\ell}K^{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{4}}{K^{1+\varepsilon}}.

Let us consider
K_{4}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
independent of
\ell
satisfying the relation
u_{\ell-1}>1+\frac{5\overline{b}u_{\ell+1}+\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}}{2K_{4}}
.
As previously, we establish that there exists a constant
C_{5}>0
such that for all
K\geqslant K_{4}
we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\cap\left\{\inf_{0\leqslant t\leqslant K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}{M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)}<u_{\ell-1}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{4{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left\langle M^{K,P_{{\rm{id}}^{2},1}}\right\rangle_{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\wedge\check{\tau}^{K}}\right)}{\left(u_{\ell-1}-1-\frac{5\overline{b}u_{\ell+1}+\underline{\theta}\underline{m}_{2}}{2K}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{5}}{K^{1+\varepsilon}}.

To conclude this proof, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
where
K_{0}:=\max_{i\in\{1,\cdots,4\}}{K_{i}}
,

{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu_{0}^{K}}\left(\left[\left\{L_{1}^{K}=\ell+1\right\}\cup\left\{T_{1}^{K}<K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\right\}\right]\cap\left\{T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right)<\frac{1}{2}-\eta_{K}

for any
\eta_{K}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon_{K}-\frac{C_{4}+C_{5}}{K^{1+\varepsilon}}
. A convenient choice is given by
\eta_{K}:=\frac{1}{2}-\frac{C}{K^{\varepsilon/2}}
for
K
large enough independent of
\ell
which completes the proof. ∎

4.4.2
Construction of the coupling

The goal of this section is to construct a coupling between
\left(L_{k}^{K}\right)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
and a biased random walk on
{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and reflected in
1
. To do this, we will construct a sequence
\left(\xi_{k}^{K}\right)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
of i.i.d. random variables with values in
\{-1,1\}
as follows.

Step 1.
First of all, thanks to Lemma
4.11
we can construct the random variable
\xi_{0}^{K}\in\left\{-1,1\right\}
such that

\left\{\xi_{0}^{K}=-1\right\}\subset\left[\left\{L_{1}^{K}=L_{0}^{K}-1\right\}\cap\left\{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\leqslant T_{1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right]\cup\left\{T_{1}^{K}\geqslant\widehat{\tau}^{K}\right\}

and
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi_{0}^{K}=-1\right)=\frac{1}{2}+\eta_{K}
.

Step 2.
Then, thanks to Lemma
4.11
again and after having applied the
Markov
property at time
T_{1}^{K}
, we can construct the random variable
\xi_{1}^{K}\in\left\{-1,1\right\}
such that, conditionally to
{\mathcal{F}}_{T_{1}^{K}}\vee\sigma\left(\xi_{0}^{K}\right)
,

\left\{\xi_{1}^{K}=-1\right\}\subset\left[\left\{L_{2}^{K}=L_{1}^{K}-1\right\}\cap\left\{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}+T_{1}^{K}\leqslant T_{2}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right]\cup\left\{T_{2}^{K}\geqslant\widehat{\tau}^{K}\right\}

and
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi_{1}^{K}=-1\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1}}}\hskip-15.6491pt\right.{\mathcal{F}}_{T_{1}^{K}}\vee\sigma\left(\xi_{0}^{K}\right)\right)=\frac{1}{2}+\eta_{K}
. Note that this implies in particular that
\xi_{1}^{K}
is independent of
\xi_{0}^{K}
.

Step 3.
By induction on
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, if
\left(\xi_{i}^{K}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k}
are constructed, then thanks to Lemma
4.11
again and after having applied the
Markov
property at time
T_{k+1}^{K}
, we can construct the random variable
\xi_{k+1}^{K}\in\left\{-1,1\right\}
such that, conditionally to
{\mathcal{F}}_{T_{k+1}^{K}}\vee\sigma\left(\xi_{0}^{K},\cdots,\xi_{k}^{K}\right)
,

\left\{\xi_{k+1}^{K}=-1\right\}\subset\left[\left\{L_{k+2}^{K}=L_{k+1}^{K}-1\right\}\cap\left\{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}+T_{k+1}^{K}\leqslant T_{k+2}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}\right\}\right]\cup\left\{T_{k+2}^{K}\geqslant\widehat{\tau}^{K}\right\}

and
{\mathbb{P}}\left(\xi_{1}^{K}=-1\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1}}}\hskip-15.6491pt\right.{\mathcal{F}}_{T_{k+1}^{K}}\vee\sigma\left(\xi_{0}^{K},\cdots,\xi_{k}^{K}\right)\right)=\frac{1}{2}+\eta_{K}
.

To conclude, we set

\forall K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star},\quad\forall k\in{\mathbb{N}},\qquad Z_{k+1}^{K}-Z_{k}^{K}:=\xi_{k}^{K}\qquad\quad{\rm and}\qquad\quad Z_{0}^{K}=L_{0}^{K}

(39)

and the sequence
\left(Z_{k}^{K}\right)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
satisfies by construction the following lemma:

Lemma 4.13
.

The sequence
\left(Z_{k}^{K}\right)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
, given by
(
39
)
is a biased simple random walk on
{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, reflected in
1
and for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
as long as
T_{k+1}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}
, that

(1)

\displaystyle Z_{k+1}^{K}-Z_{k}^{K}\geqslant L_{k+1}^{K}-L_{k}^{K}

(2)

\displaystyle T_{k+1}^{K}-T_{k}^{K}\geqslant K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\qquad{\rm when}\qquad Z_{k+1}^{K}-Z_{k}^{K}=-1.

On the one hand, note that for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
L_{k}^{K}\leqslant Z_{k}^{K}
. On the other hand, note that Lemma
4.10
implies for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
L_{k+1}^{K}-L_{k}^{K}\in\left\{\cdots,-3,-2,-1,1\right\}
which justifies (1) of Lemma
4.13
. In Figure
4.7
, we illustrate the coupling between
\left(L_{k}^{K}\right)_{0\leqslant k\leqslant 7}
and
\left(Z_{k}^{K}\right)_{0\leqslant k\leqslant 7}
. In this figure, we observe that
\xi_{k}^{K}=L_{k+1}^{K}-L_{k}^{K}
for all
k\in\left\{0,1,2,4,5,6\right\}
and
\xi_{3}^{K}=-1\geqslant-2=L_{4}^{K}-L_{3}^{K}
. This illustrates that
Z_{k+1}^{K}-Z_{k}^{K}
is always
1
when
L_{k+1}^{K}-L_{k}^{K}
is equal to
1
. Note that
Z_{k+1}^{K}-Z_{k}^{K}
can be
1
when
L_{k+1}^{K}-L_{k}^{K}
is equal to
-1
.

Time
M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)

For
K
large enough, coupling between
\left(L_{k}^{K}\right)_{0\leqslant k\leqslant 7}
and
\left(Z_{k}^{K}\right)_{0\leqslant k\leqslant 7}
up to time
T
and before
\check{\tau}^{K}
and where
M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\in[0,2u_{1}+1)
so that
L_{0}^{K}=1
and
\widetilde{Z}_{k}^{K}:=u_{Z_{k}^{K}}=3^{Z_{k}^{K}}K^{\varepsilon/2}
for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

4.4.3
Estimates of exit from an attractive domain for random walks

Let us consider
N^{K}
the number of transitions before reaching
\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor
for the random walk
\left(Z_{k}^{K}\right)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
.

Remark 4.14
.

By
Lemma
4.13
,
N^{K}\leqslant k_{0}
where
k_{0}
is the first integer such that
L_{k_{0}}^{K}=\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor
, so
\widehat{\tau}^{K}\geqslant T_{k_{0}}^{K}\geqslant T_{N^{K}}^{K}
.

The following lemma gives an estimate on the problem of exit from a domain for
Z^{K}
. For all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, we denote by
{\mathbf{P}}_{k}
the law of the
Markov
chain
Z^{K}
given
Z_{0}^{K}=L_{0}^{K}=k
.

Lemma 4.15
.

We have,

\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbf{P}}_{1}\left(N^{K}\geqslant\exp\left(\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{16\log(3)}\log^{2}(K)\right)\right)}=1.

Proof.

For all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, let us consider the stopping time
{\mathcal{T}}_{k}^{K}:=\inf\left\{n\in{\mathbb{N}}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.Z_{n}^{K}=k\right\}
and we set
v_{k}^{K}:={\mathbf{P}}_{k}\left({\mathcal{T}}_{1}^{K}<{\mathcal{T}}_{\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor}^{K}\right)
. Note that
v_{1}^{K}=1
. By a classical approach and setting
r_{K}:=\frac{1+2\eta_{K}}{1-2\eta_{K}}>1
, we prove that

\displaystyle v_{k}^{K}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-r_{K}^{-\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor-k\right)}}{1-r_{K}^{-\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor-1\right)}}
