\displaystyle=1-\left(r_{K}^{k-1}-1\right)\exp\left(-\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor-1\right)\log(r_{K})\right).

Note that
N^{K}
is greater than the number of transitions from
2
to
1
that occur before
{\mathcal{T}}_{\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor}^{K}
. Hence, under
{\mathbf{P}}_{1}
,
N^{K}\geqslant X
where
X
is random variable with geometric law of parameter
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q_{K}}:=\left(r_{K}-1\right)\exp\left(-\left(\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor-1\right)\log(r_{K})\right)
. Since
r_{K}\sim_{K\to+\infty}\frac{K^{\varepsilon/2}}{C}
, with
C>0
given by Lemma
4.11
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q_{K}\to 0}
when
K\to+\infty
and using that for all
x>0
small enough,
\log(1-x)\geqslant-2x
we deduce that for all
K
large enough,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}_{1}\left(X\geqslant\exp\left(\frac{\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor-1}{2}\log\left(r_{K}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\exp\left(\exp\left(\frac{\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor-1}{2}\log\left(r_{K}\right)\right)\log\left(1-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q_{K}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\geqslant\exp\left(-2\left(r_{K}-1\right)\exp\left(-\frac{\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor-1}{2}\log\left(r_{K}\right)\right)\right).

Using again that
r_{K}\sim_{K\to+\infty}\frac{K^{\varepsilon/2}}{C}
, we deduce that for
K
large enough,

\frac{\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\rfloor-1}{2}\log\left(r_{K}\right)\geqslant\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{16\log(3)}\log^{2}\left(K\right)

and the announced result follows. ∎

It is in the next Corollary
4.16
that we see the importance of Assumption (
5
) where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}
must not be too small.

Corollary 4.16
.

For all
T>0
,

\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}{2}\left(N^{K}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right)<T\right)}=0.

Proof.

From Lemma
4.15
and Assumption (
5
) we deduce in a straightfoward manner that

\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbf{P}}_{1}\left(\frac{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}{2}\left(N^{K}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right)<T\right)}=0.

As
L_{0}^{K}
depends on
\mu_{0}^{K}
note that
L_{0}^{K}=Z_{0}^{K}
, is function of the initial condition
\mu_{0}^{K}
. However, by Assumption (
4
) and
Markov
’s inequality, we have that

{\mathbb{P}}\left(L_{0}^{K}>1\right)\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(M_{2}\left(\mu_{0}^{K}\right)\geqslant K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\right)\leqslant\frac{C_{2}^{\star}}{K^{\varepsilon}}

which tends to
0
when
K\to+\infty
and the announced result follows. ∎

4.4.4
Conclusion

We denote respectively by
N_{+}^{K}
and
N_{-}^{K}
the number of upward transitions and downward transitions for
\left(Z_{k}^{K}\right)_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}}
before reaching
\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor
. Let
T\geqslant 0
be fixed. Note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\wedge T\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\wedge T\right\}\cap\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}>T_{N^{K}}^{K}\right\}\right)

(40)

\displaystyle+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\wedge T\right\}\cap\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}\leqslant T_{N^{K}}^{K}\right\}\right).

Step 1. Control of the first right-hand term of (
40
).
As
Z_{0}^{K}=1
with probability converging to
1
and
Z_{N^{K}}^{K}=\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor
, we have

N_{+}^{K}-N_{-}^{K}\leqslant\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor
and
N_{+}^{K}+N_{-}^{K}=N^{K}
. Thus,

N_{-}^{K}\geqslant\frac{N^{K}-\left\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right\rfloor}{2}.

Hence, if
\check{\tau}^{K}>T_{N^{K}}^{K}
, then by Lemma
4.13
(2),

\widehat{\tau}^{K}\geqslant T_{N^{K}}^{K}\geqslant\sum\limits_{i\,=\,1}^{N^{K}}{\left(T_{i}^{K}-T_{i-1}^{K}\right)}\geqslant N_{-}^{K}K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\geqslant\frac{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}{2}\left(N^{K}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right).

Therefore,

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\wedge T\right\}\cap\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}>T_{N^{K}}^{K}\right\}\right)\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}{2}\left(N^{K}-\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\right)\leqslant T\right)

which tends to
0
when
K\to+\infty
according to Corollary
4.16
.

Step 2. Control of the second right-hand term of (
40
).
If
\check{\tau}^{K}\leqslant T_{N^{K}}^{K}
, let us consider
\widetilde{N}^{K}
the last index
k
such that
T_{k}^{K}<\check{\tau}^{K}
. We have
\widetilde{N}^{K}<N^{K}
and so
\widetilde{N}^{K}+1\leqslant\widetilde{N}^{K}
which implies, thanks to Lemma
4.10
, that for all
t\leqslant T_{\widetilde{N}^{K}+1}
and
K
large enough,

M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K}\right)\leqslant 2u_{\lfloor\frac{\varepsilon}{2\log(3)}\log(K)\rfloor}+1\leqslant 2K^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}+1<K^{\varepsilon}.

As the jump times of
\nu^{K}
are isolated, we deduce that
\widehat{\tau}^{K}>T_{\widetilde{N}^{K}+1}^{K}
. Now, by definition of
\widetilde{N}^{K}
, we have
T_{\widetilde{N}^{K}+1}^{K}\geqslant\check{\tau}^{K}
and thus
\widehat{\tau}^{K}>\check{\tau}^{K}
. Hence, we obtain that

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\wedge T\right\}\cap\left\{\check{\tau}^{K}\leqslant T_{N^{K}}^{K}\right\}\right)={\mathbb{P}}\left(\varnothing\right)=0.

Therefore,
\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\widehat{\tau}^{K}\leqslant\check{\tau}^{K}\wedge T\right)}=0
which concludes the proof of Lemma
4.9
.

5
Tightness on the torus

The main result of this section is given by Theorem
5.1
. This is a stochastic averaging result inspired by
Kurtz

[
57
,
Theorem 2.1]
establishing a tightness result, in the torus case, of the sequence of laws of
\left(\left(z^{K},\Gamma^{K}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
where
\Gamma^{K}
is the occupation measure of the fast component
\mu^{K}
. We will use criteria proposed by
Ethier-Kurtz
in

[
40
,
Theorems 3.9.1 and 3.9.4]
. In Sections
6
and
7
we identify the limit and we prove its uniqueness.

Let introduce the torus
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}:=\left[x_{0}-2R,x_{0}+2R\right]
of length
4R
with
R>0
fixed and
x_{0}
is the value of the mean trait of
\nu_{0}^{K}
as in Theorem
2.1
. We define
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
the set of probability measure on
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
. We denote by
{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
the set of measures
\Gamma
on
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)
such that for all
t\geqslant 0
,
\Gamma\left([0,t]\times{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)=t
. For any
t\geqslant 0
, we denote by
{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{t}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
the set of measures
\Gamma\in{\mathcal{M}}_{m}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
restricted to
[0,t]\times{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)
. For all
T>0
, we denote by
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
the space of càd-làg functions on
[0,T]
with values in
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
.

Let
b_{R}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{2},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
and
\theta_{R}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
be two functions satisfying
b_{R}=b
on
[x_{0}-R,x_{0}+R]^{2}
and
\theta_{R}=\theta
on
[x_{0}-R,x_{0}+R]
. Let
T>0
be fixed. We define on
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
, in similar way as in Section
2
, the processes
\nu^{K,R}:=\left(\nu_{t}^{K,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
,
z^{K,R}:=\left(z_{t}^{K,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
and
\mu^{K,R}=\left(\mu_{t}^{K,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
as follows

\nu_{t}^{K,R}:=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{K}{\delta_{x_{i}^{R}(t)}},\quad z_{t}^{K,R}:=\left\langle{\rm{id}},\nu_{t/K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}^{K,R}\right\rangle\quad{\rm and}\quad\mu_{t}^{K,R}:=\left(h_{\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}}}\circ\tau_{-z_{t}^{K}}\right)\sharp\,\nu_{t/K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}^{K}

where for all
i\in\left\{1,\cdots,K\right\}
,
\nu_{t}^{K,R}
is defined on
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
from
b_{R}
and
\theta_{R}
as
\nu_{t}^{K}
was defined from
b
and
\theta
and
z_{t}^{K,R}\in{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
. Note that
\mu_{t}^{K,R}
takes values in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{K}\right)
where
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{K}
is the torus corresponding to the interval
\left[\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}}\left(x_{0}-2R-z_{t}^{K}\right),\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}\sqrt{K}}\left(x_{0}+2R-z_{t}^{K}\right)\right]
. However, we will identify in the sequel
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{K}\right)
as a subset of
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})
using the natural embedding of
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}^{K}
in
{\mathbb{R}}
. So,
\mu_{t}^{K,R}\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})
. We define also the stopping times

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}^{K,R}

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{t\geqslant 0\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right.M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K,R}\right)\geqslant K^{\varepsilon}\right\},

\displaystyle\check{\tau}^{K,R}

\displaystyle:=\inf\left\{t\geqslant 0\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right.{\rm{Diam}}\left({\rm{Supp\,}}\nu_{t}^{K,R}\right)>\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3+\varepsilon}{2}}}\right\},

and
\tau^{K,R}:=\check{\tau}^{K,R}\wedge\widehat{\tau}^{K,R}
. We define on
{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
the sequence of random measures
\left(\Gamma^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
as follows

\Gamma^{K,R}\left(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)=\delta_{\mu_{t}^{K,R}}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)\mathrm{d}t.

In the sequel, we study the limit as
K
tends to
+\infty
of the sequence of laws of

\left(\left(z^{K,R},\Gamma^{K,R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}

in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{D}}\left(\left[0,T\right],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)\right)
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
T,R>0
. The sequence of laws of
\left(\left(z^{K,R},\Gamma^{K,R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
is tight in the set of probability
measures on
{\mathbb{D}}\left(\left[0,T\right],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
and for any limiting value
{\mathbb{Q}}
of this sequence, the canonical process
\left(\zeta^{R},\Gamma^{R}\right)
on
{\mathbb{D}}\left(\left[0,T\right],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
satisfies that for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
,

N_{t}^{f,R}:=f\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)-f\left(\zeta_{0}^{R}\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm SLOW}}f\left(\zeta_{s}^{R},\mu\right)\Gamma^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}=0

(41)

{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s. Moreover, for all
t\in[0,T]
,

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{M_{5}\left(\mu\right)\Gamma^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}\right)<\infty,

(42)

so that the definition
(
41
)
makes sense.

This result is based on Propositions
3.1
and
4.8
, Lemma
4.3
. The proof is divided in eight steps. In Step 1, we establish the tightness of the family of laws of the stopped slow component
\left(z_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
. In Step 2, we establish a compact containment condition for the stopped
fast
component
\left(\mu_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
. In Step 3, we prove the tightness of the family of laws of the occupation measure. In Step 4, we deduce the tightness of the family of laws of the couple (slow, occupation measure fast). In Step 5, we prove uniform integrability results for a family
\left(N_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T],K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
constructed from
z^{K,R}
and
\Gamma^{K,R}
similarly as
N_{t}^{f,R}
in (
41
). Step 6 is devoted to establish the convergence in distribution of
\left(N_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
to
N^{f,R}
. In Steps 7 and 8, we prove that the limit
N^{f,R}
is null
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s.

The main modification of
Kurtz
’s setting of

[
57
,
Theorem 2.1]
is that we have to work with stopped times and need to be careful with moment estimates and uniform integrability properties. This led us to rewrite the proof.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Step 1. Tightness of the family of laws of
\left(z_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
on
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
.
Let
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R})
. For all
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and for all
t\in[0,T]
, let us consider the two processes
Y_{t}^{K,R}:=f\left(z_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)
and
Z_{t}^{K,R}
defined by the relation

Y_{t}^{K,R}=Y_{0}^{K,R}+\int_{0}^{t}{Z_{s}^{K,R}\mathrm{d}s}+M_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}

where
M_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}
is the martingale given by (
19
) in the torus case. Note that, for all
s>\tau^{K,R}
,
Z_{s}^{K,R}=0
. From Proposition
3.2
, (
34
) and Lemma
4.3
, note that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}{\left|Z_{t}^{K,R}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}t}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leqslant\sup_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{E}}\left[1+\int_{0}^{T}{\left|Z_{t}^{K,R}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}t}\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\leqslant C\left\{1+\sup_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{K,R}}{\left(M_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K,R}\right)+\frac{1}{K^{2}}\left[1+M_{2}^{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K,R}\right)\right]\right)\mathrm{d}t}\right]}\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt<\infty.

Hence, from

[
40
,
Theorem 3.9.4]
, the family of laws of
\left(Y^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
, on
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
, is tight. Let us observe that the compact containment condition is satisfied by the stopped
slow
component
\left(z_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
since
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
is compact and

\forall T>0,\quad\inf_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}z_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\in{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)}=1.

(43)

As
{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
is a dense subset of
{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{0}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
in the topology of the uniform norm, we deduce from (
43
) and

[
40
,
Theorem 3.9.1]
that the family of laws of
\left(z_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
is tight on
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
.

Step 2. Compact containment condition.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
R>0
. For all
T\geqslant 0
, the family of laws of the marginal random variables of the stopped
fast
process
\left(\mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T],K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
is tight on
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)
i.e.

\forall\eta>0,\ \exists\,D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta}}^{R,T}\subset{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\ {\rm compact},\ \forall t\in\left[0,T\right],\ \forall K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star},\quad{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\in D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\eta}}^{R,T}\right)\geqslant 1-\eta.

Proof.

Let
\eta>0
be fixed. From Proposition
4.8
, there exists
K_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
large enough such that for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
, for all
t\in[0,T]
,
{\mathbb{P}}\left(t>\tau^{K,R}\right)\leqslant\frac{\eta}{2}
. We consider the
{\mathbb{R}}-
valued sequence
\left(a_{q,\eta}\right)_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}}
satisfying for all
q\in{\mathbb{N}},a_{q,\eta}>1
, and
\sum_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{\frac{q}{a_{q,\eta}^{2}}}<\frac{\eta}{2\overline{M}_{2}}
where
\overline{M}_{2}:=\max\left\{C_{2}^{\star},\overline{\theta}\overline{m}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}/\underline{b}}\right\}
is a uniform upper bound of
{\mathbb{E}}\left(M_{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)\right)
given by Corollary
4.6
. Let
\left(\kappa_{q,\eta}\right)_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a sequence of compact intervals on
{\mathbb{R}}
, increasing for inclusion, of the form
\left[-a_{q,\eta},a_{q,\eta}\right]
. Let
t\in[0,T]
. For all
q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, and
K\geqslant K_{0}
, note that

M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K,R}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}}\geqslant{\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}}\int_{\kappa_{q,\eta}^{c}}{x^{2}\mu_{t}^{K,R}\left(\mathrm{d}x\right)}\geqslant a_{q,\eta}^{2}\mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\left(\kappa_{q,\eta}^{c}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}},

and we deduce that
\left\{\mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\left(\kappa_{q,\eta}^{c}\right)>\frac{1}{q}\right\}\cap\left\{t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}\right\}\subset\left\{M_{2}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\right)>\frac{a_{q,\eta}^{2}}{q}\right\}\cap\left\{t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}\right\}
. Hence,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\exists q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star},\ \mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\left(\kappa_{q,\eta}^{c}\right)>\frac{1}{q}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tau^{K,R}<t\right)+\sum_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\left(\kappa_{q,\eta}^{c}\right)>\frac{1}{q}\right\}\cap\left\{t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}\right\}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tau^{K,R}<t\right)+\sum_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K,R}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}}>\frac{a_{q,\eta}^{2}}{q}\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leqslant{\mathbb{P}}\left(\tau^{K,R}<t\right)+\sum_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{\frac{{\mathbb{E}}\left(M_{2}\left(\mu_{t}^{K,R}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}}\right)}{\frac{a_{q,\eta}^{2}}{q}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leqslant\eta.

Therefore, we have proved that

\forall\eta>0,\ \forall t\in[0,T],\ \forall K\geqslant K_{0},\quad{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta}\right)\geqslant 1-\eta,

where
{\mathcal{K}}_{\eta}:=\left\{\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right.\forall q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star},\mu\left(\kappa_{q,\eta}^{c}\right)\leqslant\frac{1}{q}\right\}
which is compact by
Prohorov
’s theorem.∎

Step 3. Tightness of the family of laws of
\left(\Gamma^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
on
{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
.
Let
\eta>0
be fixed. From Proposition
4.8
, there exists
K_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
such that for all
K\geqslant K_{0}
, for all
t\in[0,T]
,
{\mathbb{P}}\left(t\geqslant\tau^{K,R}\right)\leqslant\frac{\eta}{2}
. Consider
D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\eta}{2}}}^{R,T}
the compact set in Lemma
5.2
.
It follows that for all
t\in[0,T]
,
K\geqslant K_{0}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\Gamma^{K,R}\left([0,t]\times D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\eta}{2}}}^{R,T}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant{\mathbb{E}}\left(\Gamma^{K,R}\left([0,t]\times D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\eta}{2}}}^{R,T}\right){\mathds{1}}_{t<\tau^{K,R}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left\{\mu_{s\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\in D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\eta}{2}}}^{R,T}\right\}\cap\{t<\tau^{K,R}\}\right)\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle\geqslant\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(\mu_{s\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R}\in D_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\eta}{2}}}^{R,T}\right)\mathrm{d}s}-\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(t\geqslant\tau^{K,R}\right)\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle\geqslant t\left(1-\eta\right).

Therefore, the tightness of the family of laws of
\left(\Gamma^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
follows from

[
57
,
Lemma 1.3]
.

Step 4. Tightness of the family of laws of
\left(\left(z^{K,R},\Gamma^{K,R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
on
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
.
From Steps 1 and 3 and
Prohorov
’s theorem, we deduce that the family of laws of the couple
\left(\left(z_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,R},\Gamma^{K,R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
is relatively compact in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\right.\times

{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left.\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)\right)
. Hence, there exists a probability measure
{\mathbb{Q}}
on the canonical space
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
and an increasing function
n:{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}\to{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
such that the subsequence of laws of
\left(\left(z_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{n\left(K\right),R}}^{n\left(K\right),R},\Gamma^{n\left(K\right),R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
converges weakly to the limiting value
{\mathbb{Q}}
when
K\to+\infty
. Thanks to Proposition
4.8
, we deduce that the family of laws of
\left(\left(z^{n(K),R},\Gamma^{n(K),R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
converges weakly to
{\mathbb{Q}}
when
K\to+\infty
and therefore that the family of laws of
\left(\left(z^{K,R},\Gamma^{K,R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
is relatively compact, thus tight on
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
by
Prohorov
’s theorem.

Step 5. Uniform integrability.
For all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
,
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, let us consider
\left(N_{t}^{f,K,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
the stochastic process defined by

N_{t}^{f,K,R}:=f\left(z_{t}^{K,R}\right)-f\left(z_{0}^{K,R}\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm SLOW}f\left(z_{s}^{K,R},\mu\right)\Gamma^{K,R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}.

From (
9
), we have for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\forall t\in[0,T],\ \forall K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star},\quad\left|N_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}\right|\leqslant C\left(1+\int_{0}^{t}{M_{2}\left(\mu_{s}^{K,R}\right){\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\tau^{K,R}}\mathrm{d}s}\right),

Hence, the uniform integrability of
\left(N_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
follows from Corollary
4.4
.

Step 6. Proof of (
42
) and almost sure convergence of
\left(\widetilde{N}_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
to
\widetilde{N}^{f,R}
.
From Step 4, Proposition
4.8
and
Skorohod
’s representation theorem, there exists an increasing function
\bar{n}:{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}\to{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and a probability space on which we define, the random variable
\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}
, the families
\left(\widetilde{z}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
and
\left(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
and
\widetilde{\zeta}^{R},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{R}
copies of
\left(z^{\bar{n}(K),R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
,
\left(\Gamma^{\bar{n}(K),R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
,
\zeta^{R}
,
\Gamma^{R}
under
{\mathbb{Q}}
such that the sequence
\left(\left(\widetilde{z}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R},\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
converges a.s. to
\left(\widetilde{\zeta}^{R},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{R},+\infty\right)
when
K\to+\infty
. Note that for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\left(\left[0,t\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\right]\times{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)=t\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\xrightarrow[K\longrightarrow+\infty]{{\rm a.s.}}t=\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}\left([0,t]\times{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right).

From

[
57
,
Lemma 1.5 (b)(c)(d)]
and Corollary
4.4
, we have

\int_{0}^{t\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{K,R}}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{M_{5}\left(\mu\right)\widetilde{\Gamma}^{K,R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}\xrightarrow[K\longrightarrow+\infty]{{\rm a.s.}}\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{M_{5}\left(\mu\right)\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}

and (
42
) follows. From

[
57
,
Lemma 1.5 (b)(c)(d)]
, we also deduce that for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
, for all
t\in[0,T]
, a.s.

\displaystyle\lim_{K\to+\infty}{\int_{0}^{t\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm SLOW}f\left(\widetilde{z}_{s}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R},\mu\right)\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt=\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm SLOW}f\left(\widetilde{\zeta}^{R},\mu\right)\widetilde{\Gamma}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}

and thus the sequence
\left(\widetilde{N}_{{\tiny\bullet}\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
converges a.s. to the process
\left(\widetilde{N}_{t}^{f,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
where
\widetilde{N}_{t}^{f,K,R}
and
\widetilde{N}_{t}^{f,R}
are respectively defined for all
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
by

\widetilde{N}_{t}^{f,K,R}:=f\left(\widetilde{z}_{t}^{K,R}\right)-f\left(\widetilde{z}_{0}^{K,R}\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm SLOW}}f\left(\widetilde{z}_{t}^{K,R},\mu\right)\widetilde{\Gamma}^{K,R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}},\\
t\leqslant T\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{K,R}

\displaystyle\hskip-9.95863pt\widetilde{N}_{t}^{f,R}

\displaystyle:=f\left(\widetilde{\zeta}_{t}^{R}\right)-f\left(\widetilde{\zeta}_{0}^{R}\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{{\mathcal{L}}_{{\rm SLOW}}f\left(\widetilde{\zeta}_{t}^{R},\mu\right)\widetilde{\Gamma}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}},\quad t\leqslant T.

Step 7.
N^{f,R}
is a martingale.
Let us consider the filtration
\left(\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{t}^{K,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
defined by
\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{t}^{K,R}:=\sigma\left(\widetilde{z}_{s}^{K,R},\widetilde{\Gamma}^{K,R}\left([0,s]\times H\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.s\leqslant t,H\in{\mathcal{B}}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)\right)
,
\left(f_{i}\right)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}
,
q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
bounded
Lipschitz
functions from
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
to
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
and
0<t_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant t_{q}\leqslant s<t
. Let us denote for all
K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, for all
t\leqslant\widetilde{\tau}^{K,R}\wedge T
,
\widetilde{M}_{t}^{f,K,R}:=\widetilde{N}_{t}^{f,K,R}+\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{t}^{f,K,R}
where
\widetilde{M}^{f,K,R}
is constructed from
\widetilde{z}^{K,R}
and
\widetilde{\mu}^{K,R}
as in (
19
) and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{t}^{f,K,R}
is an error term. Note that
\widetilde{M}_{t}^{f,K,R}
is a
\left(\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{t}^{K,R}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}-
martingale as in Proposition
3.1
. Hence,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\widetilde{M}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{t\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{q}{f_{i}\left(\widetilde{M}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{t_{i}\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\right)}\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left(\widetilde{M}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{s\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{q}{f_{i}\left(\widetilde{M}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{t_{i}\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\right)}\right).

From Proposition
3.2
and (
34
),
\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{t\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}=\frac{1}{K}O\left(\int_{0}^{t}{\left[1+M_{2}\left(\widetilde{\mu}_{s}^{K,R}\right)\right]{\mathds{1}}_{s\leqslant\widetilde{\tau}^{K,R}}\mathrm{d}s}\right)
and then satisfy the condition
\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}{\left|{\mathcal{E}}_{t\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{K,R}}^{f,K,R}\right|}\right)}=0
. As for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,q\}
,
f_{i}
is
Lipschitz
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\left|f_{i}\left(\widetilde{M}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{t_{i}\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}-{\mathcal{E}}_{t_{i}\wedge\tau^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\right)-f_{i}\left(\widetilde{M}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{t_{i}\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leqslant\frac{C}{\bar{n}\left(K\right)}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{t_{i}}{\left[1+M_{2}\left(\mu_{r}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}\right)\right]{\mathds{1}}_{r\leqslant\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\mathrm{d}r}\right)

where the term of the right hand side of the previous inequality goes to
0
when
K\to+\infty
according to Lemma
4.3
. We deduce that for all
u\in\{s,t\}
,

\lim_{K\to+\infty}{\hskip-5.69046pt{\mathbb{E}}\left(\widetilde{M}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{u\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{q}{f_{i}\left(\widetilde{M}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{t_{i}\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\right)}\right)}=\lim_{K\to+\infty}{\hskip-5.69046pt{\mathbb{E}}\left(\widetilde{N}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{u\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{q}{f_{i}\left(\widetilde{N}^{f,\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}_{t_{i}\wedge\widetilde{\tau}^{\bar{n}\left(K\right),R}}\right)}\right)}.

From Steps 5 and 6, we deduce that

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\widetilde{N}^{f,R}_{t}\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{q}{f_{i}\left(\widetilde{N}^{f,R}_{t_{i}}\right)}\right)={\mathbb{E}}\left(\widetilde{N}^{f,R}_{s}\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{q}{f_{i}\left(\widetilde{N}^{f,R}_{t_{i}}\right)}\right).

Hence,

{\mathbb{E}}\left(N{}^{f,R}_{t}\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{q}{f_{i}\left(N{}^{f,R}_{t_{i}}\right)}\right)={\mathbb{E}}\left(N{}^{f,R}_{s}\prod_{i\,=\,1}^{q}{f_{i}\left(N{}^{f,R}_{t_{i}}\right)}\right).

Since the last property is true for all
q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
t_{1}\leqslant\cdots\leqslant t_{q}\leqslant s<t
and for all bounded
Lipschitz
functions
\left(f_{i}\right)_{1\leqslant i\leqslant q}
, the monotone class theorem ensures us that

{\mathbb{E}}\left(N_{t}^{f,R}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.\sigma\left(N_{u}^{f,R}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1}}}\hskip-15.6491pt\right.u\leqslant s\right)\right)=N_{s}^{f,R}.

Hence the announced result.

Step 8. Nullity of
N^{f,R}
.
On the one hand, from
Itô
’s formula

[
80
,
Theorem 32 of Chapter II]
, for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
and
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle f^{2}\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)

\displaystyle=f^{2}\left(\zeta_{0}^{R}\right)+2\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{f\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right){\mathcal{L}}_{\rm SLOW}f\left(\zeta^{R}_{s},\mu\right)\Gamma^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}

(44)

\displaystyle+2\int_{0}^{t}{f\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)\mathrm{d}N_{s}^{f,R}}+\left\langle N^{f,R}\right\rangle_{t}+\sum_{0<s\leqslant t}{\left(f\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)-f\left(\zeta_{s^{-}}^{R}\right)\right)^{2}}.

On the other hand, applying (
41
) with
f^{2}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
, we obtain that for all
t\in[0,T]

f^{2}\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)=f^{2}\left(\zeta_{0}^{R}\right)+\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm SLOW}f^{2}\left(\zeta_{s}^{R},\mu\right)\Gamma^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}+N_{t}^{f^{2},R}.

(45)

Comparing (
44
) and (
45
) leads for all
t\in[0,T]
to

N_{t}^{f^{2},R}-2\int_{0}^{t}{f\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)\mathrm{d}N_{s}^{f,R}}=\left\langle N^{f,R}\right\rangle_{t}+\sum_{0<s\leqslant t}{\left(f\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)-f\left(\zeta_{s^{-}}^{R}\right)\right)^{2}}

and thus by

[
48
,
Theorem 4.1]
that
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s. for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\left\langle N^{f,R}\right\rangle_{t}=-\sum_{0<s\leqslant t}{\left(f\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)-f\left(\zeta_{s^{-}}^{R}\right)\right)^{2}}\leqslant 0

so that
\left\langle N^{f,R}\right\rangle_{t}=0
. Hence,
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s.
N^{f,R}=0
which completes the proof. ∎

6
Characterisation of the occupation measure limit on the torus

Consider a probability measure
{\mathbb{Q}}
on
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\right)
and the canonical process
\left(\zeta^{R},\Gamma^{R}\right)
as in Theorem
5.1
. The following lemma gives us a desintegration result of the occupation measure
\Gamma^{R}
that we characterise below.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
T\geqslant 0
be fixed. With the notations of
Theorem
5.1
, there exists a random probability measure-valued process
\left(\gamma_{t}^{R}\right)_{t\in[0,T]}
that is predictable in
(\omega,t)
and such that for all bounded measurable function
\psi:[0,T]\times{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)\to{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
,

\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{\psi(s,\mu})\Gamma^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}=\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{\psi(s,\mu)\gamma^{R}_{s}(\mathrm{d}\mu)\mathrm{d}s}}.

(46)

Proof.

The desintegration result of
\Gamma^{R}
follows directly from

[
57
,
Lemma 1.4]
. ∎

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
T\geqslant 0
be fixed. With the notations of
Theorem
5.1
, we have that
\zeta^{R}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{0}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
is differentiable of derivative in
L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})

{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s.

Proof.

Applying (
41
) with
f={\rm{id}}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{1}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
, we deduce from Theorem
5.1
, (
9
) and (
46
),
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s., for all
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\zeta_{t}^{R}=\zeta_{0}^{R}+\int_{0}^{t}{\left(\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{M_{2}\left(\mu\right)\gamma_{s}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}\right)\partial_{1}{\rm{Fit}}\left(\zeta_{s}^{R},\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)\mathrm{d}s}.

From Assumptions
(A)
and (
42
), the integrand of the previous time integral is in
L^{1}({\mathbb{R}})

{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s. Hence, the announced result follows from the fundamental theorem of calculus.
∎

We now want to characterise the limiting value
\Gamma^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}t,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)=\gamma_{t}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)\mathrm{d}t
under
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Proposition 6.3
.

With the notations of
Theorem
5.1
, for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
,
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s.,
\gamma_{t}^{R}=\pi^{\lambda\left(\zeta^{R}\right)}
where
\pi^{\lambda}
is the unique invariant probability measure of the centered
Fleming-Viot
process with resampling rate
\lambda
(see

[
18
,
Section 4]
).

It is here that we exploit ergodicity properties for the fast limit component. The proof of Proposition
6.3
, given in Section
6.1
is based on the following technical lemma giving a characterisation of
\pi^{\lambda}
and proved in Section
6.2
. To state this lemma, let us first recall from (
22
) the definition of polynomials in
\mu
:

P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right):=\left\langle f,\mu^{n}\right\rangle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}{f\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\right)\mu\left(\mathrm{d}x_{1}\right)\cdots\mu\left(\mathrm{d}x_{n}\right)}}

with
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c}({\mathbb{R}})
,
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{3}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
. For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, for any function
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
whose second derivatives exist, we denote by
{\rm{Hess}}(f):=\left(\partial_{ij}^{2}f\right)_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n}
the Hessian matrix of
f
. For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, let us denote by
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{\left\|\cdot\right\|}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
the set

\displaystyle\left\{f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.\exists\,C>0,\ \forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\left.\left|f(x)\right|+\left\|\nabla f(x)\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|{\rm{Hess}}(f)(x)\right\|_{\infty}\leqslant C\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)\right\}.

From (
26
), we can see that if
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{4}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
,
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm{FVc}}^{\lambda}P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right)
is a polynomial in
\mu
of the form
P_{J,n+1}\left(\mu\right)
for some function
J\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{\left\|\cdot\right\|}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n+1},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
. We recall from

[
18
,
Proposition 2.11]
that if
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,5}({\mathbb{R}})
, then
\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(M_{5}\left(X_{t}\right)\right)}<\infty
where
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
denotes the centered
Fleming-Viot
process with resampling rate
\lambda
. Since, by (
26
), for all
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,4}({\mathbb{R}})
,

\left|{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}P_{J,n+1}(\mu)\right|\leqslant C\left(1+M_{4}\left(\mu\right)\right),

for some constant
C>0
, we can apply the martingale problem (
27
) to the function
P_{J,n+1}(\mu)
using classical localisation techniques to obtain that the process
\left(\widehat{M}_{t}^{P_{J,n+1}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
defined by

\widehat{M}_{t}^{P_{J,n+1}}:=P_{J,n+1}\left(X_{t}\right)-P_{J,n+1}\left(X_{0}\right)-\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{J,n+1}\left(X_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s}

(47)

is a
{\mathbb{P}}_{\mu}-
martingale for all
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,5}({\mathbb{R}})
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}
be fixed. If
\gamma\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})\right)
satisfies
\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{M_{4}\left(\mu\right)\gamma\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}<\infty
and

\displaystyle\forall n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star},\ \forall f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{\|\cdot\|}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right),\quad\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}{P_{f,n}}\left(\mu\right)\gamma\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}=0,

then,
\gamma=\pi^{\lambda}
.

6.1
Proof of Proposition
6.3

Let us define for all
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
{\mathscr{C}}_{K}^{\ell}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
the space of real functions of class
{\mathscr{C}}^{\ell}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
with compact support. From Lemma
3.4
and (
13
), for all
t\in[0,T]
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
we have for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{K}^{3}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
that

\displaystyle K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}M_{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}^{K,P_{f,n}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\,=\,1}^{3}{{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{i}\left(t\wedge\tau^{K,R}\right)}

where for all
t\leqslant\tau^{K,R}\wedge T

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{1}(t)

\displaystyle:=K^{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}\left(P_{f,n}\left(\mu_{t}^{K,R}\right)-P_{f,n}\left(\mu_{0}^{K,R}\right)\right),

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{2}(t)

\displaystyle:=-\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{\theta\left(z_{s}^{K,R}\right)m_{2}\left(z_{s}^{K,R}\right){\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda\left(z_{s}^{K,R}\right)}P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right)\Gamma^{K,R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}},

\displaystyle{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{3}(t)

\displaystyle:=O\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{K}}+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K^{\frac{3}{2}+\varepsilon}+\int_{0}^{t}{\frac{M_{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K,R}\right)}{K}\,\mathrm{d}s}\right),

is a martingale. Since
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}}K\to 0
by Assumption (
5
),
{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{1}\left(t\wedge\tau^{K,R}\right)\to 0
when
K\to+\infty
. From Corollary
4.4
, the sequence
\left({\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{2}\left(t\wedge\tau^{K}\right)\right)_{t\in[0,T],K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
is uniformly integrable and converges in law, when
K\to+\infty
, to

M_{t}^{P_{f,n}}:=-\int_{0}^{t}{\theta\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)m_{2}\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)}P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right)\Gamma^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}s,\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}}.

Note that, from
Cauchy-Schwarz
’s inequality and Lemma
4.3
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}{\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau^{K,R}}{\frac{M_{3}\left(\mu_{s}^{K,R}\right)}{K}}}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{2}\leqslant\frac{T}{K^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau^{K,R}}{M_{6}\left(\mu_{s}^{K,R}\right)\mathrm{d}s}\right)\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{CT^{2}}{K^{2}}}.

Hence,
\lim_{K\to+\infty}{\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}{{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{3}(t\wedge\tau^{K,R})}}=0
in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})
. In particular, we deduce that a subsequence of
\left(\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}{{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{3}(t\wedge\tau^{K,R})}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
converges almost surely to
0
and the family
\left(\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant T}{{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(A)}}}}}^{K,R}_{3}(t\wedge\tau^{K,R})}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
is uniformly integrable along this subsequence

[
84
,
Theorem 13.7]
. Using the same method based on
Skorohod
’s representation theorem as in the proof of Theorem
5.1
, we deduce that the process
\left(M_{t}^{P_{f,n}}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is a
{\mathbb{Q}}-
martingale. As it is also a continuous and finite variation process by Lemma
46
, it must hence be
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s. null

[
48
,
Theorem 4.1]
. Hence, using Lemma
46
again, we have proved that

\forall f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{K}^{3}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right),\ {\mathbb{Q}}-{\rm a.s.},\ \mathrm{d}t-{\rm a.e.},\quad\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)}P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right)\gamma_{t}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}=0.

The space
{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
equipped with the norm

\left\|f\right\|_{W_{0}^{2,\infty}}=\left\|f\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\nabla f\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|{\rm{Hess}}(f)\right\|_{\infty}

is separable. So, we can choose a dense countable family
{\mathcal{B}}\subset{\mathscr{C}}^{3}_{K}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
such that

\forall f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{K}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right),\ \exists\,\left(f_{q}\right)_{q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}\in{\mathcal{B}}^{{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}},\quad f_{q}\xrightarrow[q\longrightarrow+\infty]{\|\cdot\|_{W_{0}^{2,\infty}}}f.

Then,

{\mathbb{Q}}-{\rm a.s.},\ \mathrm{d}t-{\rm a.e.},\ \forall q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star},\quad\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)}P_{f_{q},n}\left(\mu\right)\gamma_{t}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}=0.

From (
42
) and Lemma
46
, we have that
\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{M_{4}\left(\mu\right)\gamma_{t}^{R}(\mathrm{d}\mu)}<\infty
. As for all
q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,

{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)}P_{f_{q},n}\left(\mu\right)\leqslant C_{1}\left(1+M_{2}\left(\mu\right)\right)\left\|f_{q}\right\|_{W_{0}^{2,\infty}}.

for some constant
C_{1}>0
, we obtain by the dominated convergence theorem that

{\mathbb{Q}}-{\rm a.s.},\ \mathrm{d}t-{\rm a.e.},\ \forall f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{K}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right),\quad\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)}P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right)\gamma_{t}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}=0.

(48)

Let us consider
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{\|\cdot\|}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
and for all
q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
x\mapsto\chi_{q}(x)=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{q^{2}-\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2}}\right){\mathds{1}}_{\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}<q}
of class
{\mathscr{C}}^{\infty}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
with compact support. Then, for all
q\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
f\chi_{q}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{K}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
. Noting that,
\left|\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)}P_{f\chi_{q},n}\left(\mu\right)\gamma_{t}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}\right|
is dominated by
C_{2}\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{\left(1+M_{4}\left(\mu\right)\right)\gamma_{t}^{R}(\mathrm{d}\mu)}
for some constant
C_{2}>0
, and since
\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{M_{4}\left(\mu\right)\gamma_{t}^{R}(\mathrm{d}\mu)}<\infty
, we deduce from (
48
) applied to
f\chi_{q}
, by the dominated convergence theorem, that

{\mathbb{Q}}-{\rm a.s.},\ \mathrm{d}t-{\rm a.e.},\ \forall f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{\|\cdot\|}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right),\quad\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)}P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right)\gamma_{t}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}=0.

Therefore, it follows from Lemma
6.4
that
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s,
\mathrm{d}t-
a.e.,
\gamma_{t}^{R}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)=\pi^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)
which concludes the proof.

6.2
Proof of Lemma
6.4

In Section
6.2.1
we extend some duality results for the centered
Fleming-Viot
process, obtained in

[
18
]
, which are be useful to prove Lemma
6.4
in Section
6.2.2
.

6.2.1
Extension of the duality result for the centered
Fleming-Viot
process

Let us recall that the dual process
\left(\xi_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
of the centered
Fleming-Viot
process
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
with resampling rate
\lambda
, on the state space
\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{{\mathscr{C}}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)}
, obtained in

[
18
,
Section 3.2]
is defined as below.

Let us consider
M:=\left(M(t)\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
a
Markov
’s birth and death process in
{\mathbb{N}}
whose transition rates
q_{i,j}
from
i
to
j
are given by:

{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(1)}}}}}\ \ q_{n,n+1}=\lambda n^{2}\qquad\quad{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(2)}}}}}\ \ q_{n,n-1}=\lambda n(n-1)\qquad\quad{\rm{\textbf{\rm{{(3)}}}}}\ \ q_{i,j}=0\ {\rm{otherwise}}.

For all
M(0)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
and
\lambda>0
, we define

\xi_{t}:=T_{\lambda}^{\left(M\left(\tau_{n}\right)\right)}\left(t-\tau_{n}\right)\Lambda_{n}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M\left(\tau_{n-1}\right)\right)}\left(\tau_{n}-\tau_{n-1}\right)\Lambda_{n-1}\cdots\Lambda_{1}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M\left(0\right)\right)}\left(\tau_{1}\right)\xi_{0},\\
\tau_{n}\leqslant t<\tau_{n+1},\ n\in{\mathbb{N}},

(49)

where
\left(\tau_{n}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is the sequence of jump times of
M
with
\tau_{0}=0
,
\left(T_{\lambda}^{(n)}(t)\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is the semi-group of operator associated to the generator
B^{(n)}_{\lambda}
given by (
23
) and where
\left(\Lambda_{n}\right)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
is a sequence of random operators. These are conditionally independent given
M
and satisfy for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 1
and
1\leqslant i\neq j\leqslant n
,

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\Lambda_{k}=\Phi_{i,j}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.\left\{M\left(\tau_{k}^{-}\right)=n,M\left(\tau_{k}\right)=n-1\right\}\right)=\frac{1}{n(n-1)}

and for all
n\geqslant 1
and
1\leqslant i,j\leqslant n
,

{\mathbb{P}}\left(\Lambda_{k}=K_{i,j}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-19.91684pt\right.\left\{M\left(\tau_{k}^{-}\right)=n,M\left(\tau_{k}\right)=n+1\right\}\right)=\frac{1}{n^{2}},

where
\Phi_{i,j}
and
K_{i,j}
are respectively defined in (
24
) and (
25
). Note that if
M(0)=1
, the dual process can only jump from
\xi_{0}
to
K_{ij}\xi_{0}
. As for all
t\geqslant 0
,
\xi_{t}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(t)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
,
K_{i,j}\xi_{t}
is well defined. Moreover, the dual process
\left(\xi_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
with initial condition
\xi_{0}
is constructed on the same probability space and independently of the centered
Fleming-Viot
process
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
with resampling rate
\lambda
and initial condition
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
. We shall denote by
{\mathbb{P}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}
, the law of the couple
\left(\left(X_{t},\xi_{t}\right)\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
on this probability space.

We denote by
S_{t}
the number of jumps of the process
M
on
[0,t]
. We start with a result giving bounds on the dual process, which is an extension of similar estimates obtained in

[
18
,
Lemma 6.4]
.

Lemma 6.5
.

For all
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
there exists
C_{0}:\bigcup_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}{(0,+\infty)^{k-1}\times\{k\}}\to{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
, locally bounded, such that

C_{0}\left(\tau_{1},1\right):=\left\|\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{3}{M(0)}\left\|{\rm{Hess}}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

and, for all
\left(t_{j}\right)_{j\in{\mathbb{N}}}\in(0,+\infty)^{{\mathbb{N}}}
,
k\mapsto C_{0}\left(\left(t_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k\right)
is non-decreasing and satisfying

\displaystyle\forall k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star},\ \forall t\leqslant\tau_{k},\ \forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(t)},

(50)

\displaystyle\left|\xi_{t}(x)\right|\leqslant C_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k\right)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2S_{t}}\right).

Proof.

By induction on
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, we prove the property

\displaystyle({\mathcal{Q}}_{k}):\quad

\displaystyle\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k}\right)},\quad\left|\xi_{\tau_{k}}(x)\right|\leqslant C_{0}(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2k}\right).

Step 1. Initial case: computation of
C_{0}\left(\tau_{1},1\right)
.
Let us recall some notations of

[
18
,
Theorem 6.1]
. For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, we denote by
\bm{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, the vector whose coordinates are all
1
. For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
t\geqslant 0
,
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
and
\lambda>0
, let us consider
g_{t,x}^{\lambda}
the density of the Gaussian distribution
{\mathcal{N}}^{(n)}\left(m_{t,x}^{\lambda},\Sigma_{t}^{\lambda}\right)
where
m_{t,x}^{\lambda}:=x-\frac{\left(1-\exp\left(-2\lambda nt\right)\right)}{n}(x\cdot\bm{1})\bm{1}
and
\Sigma_{t}^{\lambda}:=P\sigma_{t}^{\lambda}P^{-1}
with

\sigma_{t}^{\lambda}:=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1-\exp\left(-4\lambda nt\right)}{4\lambda n}&0&\dots&\dots&0\vskip 2.84544pt\\
0&t&0&\dots&0\\
\vdots&0&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&0\\
0&0&\dots&0&t\end{pmatrix}\quad{\rm and}\quad P:=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}&\cdots&\cdots&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}\\
\vdots&-\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}}&\ddots&\cdots&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}\\
\vdots&0&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&\frac{1}{\sqrt{n(n-1)}}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}&0&\cdots&0&-\sqrt{\frac{n-1}{n}}\end{pmatrix}.

Note that for all
i,j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
,
\partial_{x_{i}}m_{t,x}^{\lambda}=\epsilon_{i}-\frac{1-\exp\left(-2\lambda nt\right)}{n}\bm{1}
and
\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}^{2}m_{t,x}^{\lambda}=0
where
\left(\epsilon_{1},\cdots,\epsilon_{n}\right)
is the canonical basis of
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. For all
\lambda>0
, the key identity for the sequel is

\forall f\in L^{\infty}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\right),\ \forall t\geqslant 0,\ \forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\quad T_{\lambda}^{(M(0))}(t)f(x)=\left(f\ast g_{t,0}^{\lambda}\right)\left(m_{t,x}^{\lambda}\right)

(51)

where
\ast
stands for the convolution product (see (46) in

[
18
,
Theorem 6.1]
).

On the one hand, note that for all
t<\tau_{1}
,
M(t)=M(0)
. In this case, for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)}
,
\lambda>0
,
\xi_{t}(x)=T_{\lambda}^{\left(M(0)\right)}(t)\xi_{0}(x)
and so, from

[
18
,
Theomem 6.1]
,
\left|\xi_{t}(x)\right|\leqslant\left\|\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}
. On the other hand, at time
\tau_{1}
, we make a partition of cases according to whether the dual process loses or gains a variable. Let
i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,M(0)\right\}
be fixed.

•

Case
\Lambda_{1}=\Phi_{i,j}
.
In this case,
M(\tau_{1})=M(0)-1
and we deduce from (
49
) that

\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)-1},\qquad\xi_{\tau_{1}}(x)=\Phi_{i,j}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M(0)\right)}\left(\tau_{1}\right)\xi_{0}(x).

By (
24
), we deduce that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)-1}
,
\left|\xi_{\tau_{1}}(x)\right|\leqslant\left\|\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}
.

•

Case
\Lambda_{1}=K_{i,j}
.
In this case,
M(\tau_{1})=M(0)+1
and we deduce from (
49
) and (
25
) then from

[
18
,
Theorem 6.1
(3)
]
and properties of the convolution product that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
,

\displaystyle\xi_{\tau_{1}}(x)

\displaystyle=K_{i,j}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M(0)\right)}\left(\tau_{1}\right)\xi_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M(0)\right)}\left(\tau_{1}\right)\xi_{0}\left(\widetilde{x}\right)x_{M(0)+1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\partial_{x_{j}}m_{\tau_{1},\widetilde{x}}^{\lambda}\right)^{t}\left[\left({\rm{Hess}}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\ast g_{\tau_{1},0}^{\lambda}\right)\left(m_{\tau_{1},\widetilde{x}}^{\lambda}\right)\partial_{x_{i}}m_{t,\widetilde{x}}^{\lambda}\right]x_{M(0)+1}^{2}

where
\widetilde{x}=\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{M(0)}\right)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)}
. As,

\left({\rm{Hess}}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\ast g_{\tau_{1},0}^{\lambda}\right)\left(m_{\tau_{1},\widetilde{x}}^{\lambda}\right)=\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)}}{\partial_{ij}^{2}\xi_{0}(u)g_{\tau_{1},0}^{\lambda}\left(m_{\tau_{1},\widetilde{x}}^{\lambda}-u\right)\mathrm{d}u}\right)_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant M(0)}

we obtain that
\left\|\left({\rm{Hess}}\left(\xi_{0}\right)\ast g_{\tau_{1},0}^{\lambda}\right)\left(m_{\tau_{1},\widetilde{x}}^{\lambda}\right)\right\|_{\infty}\leqslant\left\|\partial_{ij}^{2}\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}
. Noting that

\left|\left(\partial_{x_{j}}m_{\tau_{1},\widetilde{x}}^{\lambda}\right)^{t}\partial_{x_{i}}m_{t,\widetilde{x}}^{\lambda}\right|\leqslant\frac{3}{M(0)},

we deduce that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
,

\left|\xi_{\tau_{1}}(x)\right|\leqslant\frac{3}{M(0)}\sup_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant M(0)}{\left\|\partial_{ij}^{2}\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}}x_{M(0)+1}^{2}\leqslant\frac{3}{M(0)}\left\|{\rm{Hess}}(\xi_{0})\right\|_{\infty}\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2}

and
({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
follows.

Step 2. Inductive Step.
We assume that, for
k\in{\mathbb{N}}\,\backslash\,\{0,1\}
,
({\mathcal{Q}}_{k-1})
is satisfied and prove that
({\mathcal{Q}}_{k})
is also. We make again a partition of cases according to whether the dual process loses or gains a variable. Let
i,j\in\left\{1,\cdots,M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right\}
be fixed.

•

Case
\Lambda_{k}=\Phi_{i,j}
at the
k^{\rm{th}}
jump.
In this case,
M(\tau_{k})=M(\tau_{k-1})-1
and we deduce from (
49
) that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)-1}
,
\xi_{\tau_{k}}(x)=\Phi_{i,j}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M(\tau_{k-1})\right)}(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1})\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x)
.
By using (
24
) and
\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{k-1}\right)
, we deduce from

[
18
,
Corollary 6.2
(1)
]
that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)-1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\Phi_{i,j}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right)}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1}\right)\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{2}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right){C}_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k\right)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2(k-1)}\right),

where
C_{2}{C}_{0}
is locally bounded. As
t\mapsto C_{2}\left(t,M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right)
is non-decreasing, we deduce
\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{k}\right)
in that case.

•

Case
\Lambda_{k}=K_{i,j}
at the
k^{\rm{th}}
jump.

In this case,
M(\tau_{k})=M(\tau_{k-1})+1
. From (
49
), (
25
) and

[
18
,
Theorem 6.1
(3)
]
, we have for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1}
,

\displaystyle\left|\xi_{\tau_{k}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|K_{i,j}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1\right)}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1}\right)\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left(\partial_{x_{j}}m^{\lambda}_{\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},\widetilde{x}}\right)^{t}\left[\left(\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}\ast{\rm{Hess}}\left(g_{\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},0}^{\lambda}\right)\right)(m^{\lambda}_{\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},\widetilde{x}})\partial_{x_{i}}m^{\lambda}_{\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},\widetilde{x}}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times x_{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1}^{2},

where
\widetilde{x}=\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)}\right)^{t}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)}
. From
\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{k-1}\right)
and

[
18
,
Corollary 6.2
(2)
]
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|K_{i,j}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)+1\right)}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1}\right)\xi_{\tau_{k-1}}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{3}\left(\tau_{k}-\tau_{k-1},M\left(\tau_{k-1}\right)\right)C_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k\right)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2k}\right),

where
C_{3}{C}_{0}
is locally bounded and
\left({\mathcal{Q}}_{k}\right)
follows in that case.

We conclude by the principle of induction.

Step 3. Proof of (
50
) for
t<\tau_{k}
.
Note that from (
49
), for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
t\in\,\left(\tau_{k},\tau_{k+1}\right)
,

\forall x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M\left(t\right)},\qquad\xi_{t}(x)=T_{\lambda}^{\left(M\left(\tau_{k}\right)\right)}\left(t-\tau_{k}\right)\xi_{\tau_{k}}(x),

so the announced result follows from

[
18
,
Corollary 6.2
(1)
]
. ∎

Let us consider for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
\ell,m\in{\mathbb{N}}
the stopping times

\displaystyle\vartheta_{k,\ell}

\displaystyle:=\inf{\left\{t\geqslant 0\left.\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right|S_{t}\geqslant k\quad{\rm{or}}\quad\exists s\in[0,t],\ \left\langle\xi_{s},X_{t-s}^{M(s)}\right\rangle\geqslant\ell\right\}},

\displaystyle\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\inf{\left\{t\geqslant 0\left.\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right|S_{t}\geqslant k\quad{\rm{or}}\quad C_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k\right)\geqslant m\right\}},

\displaystyle\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}

\displaystyle:=\inf{\left\{t\geqslant 0\left.\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\hskip-17.07182pt\right|S_{t}\geqslant k\quad{\rm{or}}\quad\exists s\in[0,t],\ \left\langle\xi_{s},X_{t-s}^{M(s)}\right\rangle\geqslant\ell\right.}

\displaystyle\hskip 83.93553pt\left.\phantom{1^{1^{1^{1}}}}\quad{\rm{or}}\quad C_{0}\left(\left(\tau_{i+1}-\tau_{i}\right)_{0\leqslant i\leqslant k-1},k\right)\geqslant m\right\}.

As
\left(M(t),\xi_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is independent of
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
, note that for all
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}
is independent of
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
. Recall that for all
t\geqslant 0
,
\xi_{t}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(t)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
. For all
n\geqslant M(t)
, for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, we denote by
\xi_{t}^{(n)}(x)=\xi_{t}^{(n)}\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\right):=\xi_{t}\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{M(t)}\right)
so that for all
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
,

\left\langle\xi_{t}^{(n)},\mu^{n}\right\rangle=\left\langle\xi_{t},\mu^{M\left(t\right)}\right\rangle.

The next lemma is an extension of the duality identity proved in

[
18
]
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Given
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
,
\left(\xi_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
as above with
X_{0}:=\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2k}({\mathbb{R}})
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
and
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
, we have that
{\mathbb{P}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}-
a.s., for all
t\geqslant 0
, for all
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\langle\xi_{0},X_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}^{M(0)}\right\rangle\right)=\left\langle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi_{0}}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right),\mu^{M(0)+k}\right\rangle.

(52)

Compared to the duality identity of

[
18
]
, the key point of this lemma is that the right-hand side of the last equation, is a polynomial in
\mu
.

Proof.

Let us consider
t\geqslant 0
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
\ell,m\in{\mathbb{N}}
. As
\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}\leqslant\vartheta_{k,\ell}
, the weak duality identity of

[
18
,
Theorem 3.4]
implies that:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\langle\xi_{0},X_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{M(0)}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\langle\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}},\mu^{M\left(t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}\right)}\right\rangle\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\langle\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)},\mu^{M(0)+k}\right\rangle\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right).

Now, from Lemma
50
and the definition of
\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}
, note that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+k}
,

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}(x)\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)

(53)

\displaystyle\leqslant m\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2k}\right)\exp\left(\lambda t\left(M(0)+k\right)^{2}\right).

Since
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2k}({\mathbb{R}})
, we deduce from
Fubini
’s theorem that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\langle\xi_{0},X_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{M(0)}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\left\langle{\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right),\mu^{M(0)+k}\right\rangle.

On the one hand, from

[
18
,
Section 6.2.1]
,
\lim_{\ell\to+\infty}{\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}=\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}

{\mathbb{P}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}-
a.s., and since
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
has continous paths for the topology of weak convergence, we have
{\mathbb{P}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}-
a.s.

\lim_{\ell\to+\infty}{\left\langle\xi_{0},X_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{M(0)}\right\rangle}=\left\langle\xi_{0},X_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}^{M(0)}\right\rangle.

Therefore, we deduce from the
Lebesgue
dominated convergence theorem, that for all
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
,

\lim_{\ell\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\langle\xi_{0},X_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{M(0)}\right\rangle\right)}={\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\langle\xi_{0},X_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}^{M(0)}\right\rangle\right).

On the other hand, Lemma
50
and the dominated convergence theorem imply that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+k}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}(x)\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt={\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}(x)\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)

and the limit satisfies the inequality (
53
).
As
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2k}({\mathbb{R}})
, we deduce again from the dominated convergence theorem that

\displaystyle\lim_{\ell\to+\infty}{\left\langle{\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,\ell,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right),\mu^{M(0)+k}\right\rangle}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt=\left\langle{\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right),\mu^{M(0)+k}\right\rangle

and the announced result follows from the fact that
\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}
is independent of
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
, which implies

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}(x)\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt={\mathbb{E}}_{\xi_{0}}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}^{\left(M(0)+k\right)}(x)\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\vartheta_{k,m}^{\prime}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right).

∎

From Lemma
50
, we deduce that we can choose
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
large enough such that
\vartheta^{\prime}_{1,m}=\tau_{1}
.
In this case, the function inside the brackets in the left-hand side of (
52
) is defined, for all
t\geqslant 0
and for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
, by

\psi_{t}(x):={\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\xi_{t\wedge\tau_{1}}^{\left(M(0)+1\right)}(x)\exp\left(\lambda\int_{0}^{t\wedge\tau_{1}}{M^{2}(u)\mathrm{d}u}\right)\right).

For all
t\geqslant 0
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
,
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
and
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
let us denote

P_{\rm{FVc}}(t)P_{f,n}\left(\mu\right):={\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(P_{f,n}\left(X_{t}\right)\right)

the semi-group of the centered
Fleming-Viot
process
(X_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
with resampling rate
\lambda
.

The following result is the main result of this section. It gives an extension of Lemma
50
to a function which appears naturally in the proof of Lemma
6.4
.

Proposition 6.7
.

For all
t\geqslant 0
, for all
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
, for all
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)

P_{\rm{FVc}}(t)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)=P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}\left(\mu\right):=\left\langle V_{t},\mu^{M(0)+1}\right\rangle

where

V_{t}:=\exp\left(\alpha_{0}t\right)\psi_{t}-\alpha_{0}\int_{0}^{t}{\exp\left(\alpha_{0}s\right)\psi_{s}\mathrm{d}s}

(54)

and
\alpha_{0}:=\lambda M(0)\left[2M(0)-1\right]
. In addition, for all
t\geqslant 0
, there exist a constant
C_{t}>0
such that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
,

\left|V_{t}(x)\right|\leqslant C_{t}\left(1+\|x\|^{2}_{\infty}\right).

Proof.

Let
t\geqslant 0
,
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
,
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
be fixed. Note that from Lemma
50
, there exists a constant
\widetilde{C}_{t}
, depending only on
\xi_{0}
and
t
such that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
,

\left|\psi_{t}(x)\right|\leqslant C_{0}\left(\tau_{1},1\right)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right){\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\exp\left(\lambda tM^{2}(0)\right)\right)\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{t}\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right).

(55)

Now, from Lemma
52
and the fact that
\tau_{1}
is independent of
\left(X_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\left\langle\psi_{t},\mu^{M(0)+1}\right\rangle={\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{t\wedge\tau_{1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\xi_{0}}\left(P_{\rm{FVc}}\left(t\wedge\tau_{1}\right)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(\mu\right)\right).

As
\tau_{1}
follows an exponential law of parameter
\alpha_{0}
we have that

\displaystyle\left\langle\psi_{t},\mu^{M(0)+1}\right\rangle=P_{\rm{FVc}}\left(t\right)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(\mu\right)\exp\left(-\alpha_{0}t\right)

(56)

\displaystyle+\alpha_{0}\int_{0}^{t}{P_{\rm{FVc}}\left(s\right)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(\mu\right)\exp\left(-\alpha_{0}s\right)\mathrm{d}s}.

Thanks to (
55
) and since
\mu
has its moment of order 2 finite, the
Fubini
theorem ensures us that

\left\langle\int_{0}^{t}{\exp\left(\alpha_{0}s\right)\psi_{s}\mathrm{d}s},\mu^{M(0)+1}\right\rangle=\int_{0}^{t}{\exp\left(\alpha_{0}s\right)\left\langle\psi_{s},\mu^{M(0)+1}\right\rangle\mathrm{d}s}.

From (
56
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\langle\int_{0}^{t}{\exp\left(\alpha_{0}s\right)\psi_{s}\mathrm{d}s},\mu^{M(0)+1}\right\rangle

(57)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{0}\int_{0}^{t}{\exp\left(\alpha_{0}s\right)\int_{0}^{s}{\exp\left(-\alpha_{0}u\right)P_{\rm{FVc}}(u)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\mathrm{d}u}\mathrm{d}s}+\int_{0}^{t}{P_{\rm{FVc}}(s)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)\mathrm{d}s}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\alpha_{0}t\right)\int_{0}^{t}{\exp\left(-\alpha_{0}u\right)P_{\rm{FVc}}(u)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)\mathrm{d}u}.

Then, the first announced result follows from (
56
) and (
57
) and the second one from (
55
). ∎

6.2.2
Proof of Lemma
6.4

The key point of this proof is to establish that for all
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{4}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
,
t\geqslant 0
and
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,4}({\mathbb{R}})
,

\displaystyle P_{\rm FVc}(t)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\xi_{0},\mu^{M(0)}\right\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\rm FVc}(s)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)\mathrm{d}s}

(58)

\displaystyle=P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)+\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{V_{s},M(0)+1}(\mu)\mathrm{d}s}.

where we use Proposition
6.7
in the second equality. To do this, we will first prove that for all
t\geqslant 0
, there exists a constant
C_{t}>0
such that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
,

\left\|{\rm{Hess}}\left(V_{t}\right)(x)\right\|_{\infty}\leqslant C_{t}\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)
, so that
V_{t}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{\|\cdot\|}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
.

For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}
, for any function
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
times differentiable, we denote by
D^{k}f
the differential of order
k
of
f
. Let
t\geqslant 0
,
\lambda>0
,
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,2}({\mathbb{R}})
and
\xi_{0}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{4}_{b}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
be fixed.

Step 1. Preliminary bounds.
Let
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)}
. It follows from (
51
) that

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{i}x_{j}x_{k}x_{\ell}}^{4}T^{\left(M(0)\right)}(t)\xi_{0}(x)

\displaystyle\hskip 7.11317pt=\sum_{m,p,q,r\,=\,1}^{M(0)}\partial_{x_{i}}\left(m_{t,x}^{\lambda}\right)_{m}\partial_{x_{j}}\left(m_{t,x}^{\lambda}\right)_{p}\partial_{x_{k}}\left(m_{t,x}^{\lambda}\right)_{q}\partial_{x_{\ell}}\left(m_{t,x}^{\lambda}\right)_{r}\left(g_{t,0}^{\lambda}\ast\partial_{y_{m}y_{p}y_{q}y_{r}}^{4}{\xi_{0}}\right)\left(m_{t,x}^{\lambda}\right)

where
\left(m_{t,x}^{\lambda}\right)_{p}
designates the
p^{\rm th}
component of
m_{t,x}^{\lambda}
. Then, for all
k\in\left\{0,\cdots,4\right\}
, there exists a constant
C_{1}>0
independent of
x
and
t
such that

\left\|D^{k}T^{\left(M(0)\right)}(t)\xi_{0}(x)\right\|_{\infty}\leqslant C_{1}\left(1+\left\|D^{k}\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}\right).

From (
49
), (
24
) and (
25
), we deduce that there exists a constant
C_{2}>0
independent of
t
such that for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
, for all
k\in\{0,1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}\xi_{t\wedge\tau_{1}}^{\left(M(0)+1\right)}(x)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{1}\left(1+\left\|D^{k}\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}\right)+\sum_{i,j\,=\,1}^{n}{\left\|D^{k}K_{i,j}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M(0)\right)}\left(\tau_{1}\right)\xi_{0}(x)\right\|_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\,=\,1\\
i\,\neq\,j\end{subarray}}^{n}{\left\|D^{k}\Phi_{i,j}T_{\lambda}^{\left(M(0)\right)}\left(\tau_{1}\right)\xi_{0}\left(x_{1},\cdots,x_{M(0)-1}\right)\right\|_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{2}\left(1+\left\|D^{k}\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|D^{k+2}\xi_{0}\right\|_{\infty}x_{M(0)+1}^{2}\right).

By the theorem of differentiation under the integral sign, for all
k\in\{0,1,2\}
and
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|D^{k}\psi_{t}(x)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leqslant\exp\left(\lambda M(0)^{2}t\right){\mathbb{E}}_{\left(\mu,\xi_{0}\right)}\left(\left\|D^{k}\xi_{t\wedge\tau_{1}}^{\left(M(0)+1\right)}(x)\right\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{2}\exp\left(\lambda M(0)^{2}t\right)\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right).

Then, from the definition of
V_{t}
in (
54
) we deduce that there exists a constant
C_{t}
such that for all
k\in\{0,1,2\}
, for all
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1}
,
\left\|D^{k}V_{t}(x)\right\|_{\infty}\leqslant C_{t}\left(1+\left\|x\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)
, so that
V_{t}\in{\mathscr{C}}^{2}_{\|\cdot\|}\left({\mathbb{R}}^{M(0)+1},{\mathbb{R}}\right)
.

Step 2. Proof of the key point (
58
).
Thanks to (
27
), for all
t\geqslant 0
,

P_{\rm FVc}(t)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)=P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)+{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s}\right).

From (
26
), there exists a constant
C_{3}>0
such that for all
t\geqslant 0
,
{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(\left|{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{t}\right)\right|\right)\leqslant C_{3}\left(1+{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(M_{2}\left(X_{t}\right)\right)\right)
which is finite from

[
18
,
Proposition 2.11]
since
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,4}({\mathbb{R}})
. Hence, from
Fubini
’s theorem we obtain that

P_{\rm FVc}(t)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)=P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)+\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{s}\right)\right)\mathrm{d}s}.

(59)

As
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)
is a polynomial in
\mu
and since

\left|{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)\right)\right|\leqslant C_{4}\left(1+M_{4}\left(\mu\right)\right)

for some constant
C_{4}>0
, we deduce as in (
47
) that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{t}\right)\right)-{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt={\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(\int_{0}^{t}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\right)\left(X_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s}\right).

In particular,
t\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{t}\right)\right)
is continuous, so

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{h\to 0}{\frac{1}{h}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(\int_{t}^{t+h}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s}\right)}

\displaystyle=\lim_{h\to 0}\frac{1}{h}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{t+h}\right)-P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{h\to 0}{\frac{1}{h}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(P_{\rm{FVc}}\left(t\right)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{h}\right)\right)-P_{\rm{FVc}}\left(t\right)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\lim_{h\to 0}\frac{1}{h}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}\left(X_{h}\right)-P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}\left(\mu\right)\right)

where we used
Markov
’s property in the third equality and Proposition
6.7
in the last equality. From Step 1, we deduce that there exists a constant
C_{5}>0
such that

\left|{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}(\mu)\right|\leqslant C_{5}\left(1+M_{4}\left(\mu\right)\right).

(60)

Thus, from the martingale problem (
47
) and the continuity of
t\mapsto{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}P_{V_{t},M(0)}\left(\mu\right)\right)
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{h\to 0}\frac{1}{h}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}\left(X_{h}\right)-P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}\left(\mu\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\lim_{h\to 0}{\frac{1}{h}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left(\int_{0}^{h}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}\left(X_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s}\right)}

\displaystyle={\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}{P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}\left(\mu\right)}.

Therefore, we have proved that for all
t\geqslant 0
, for all
\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}_{1}^{c,4}({\mathbb{R}})
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\mu}\left({\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}\left(X_{t}\right)\right)={\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}P_{V_{t},M(0)+1}(\mu),

and so, by (
59
), we obtain (
58
).

Step 3. Conclusion.
From (
58
) and (
60
) and since
\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{M_{4}\left(\mu\right)\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mu)}<\infty
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{P_{\rm FVc}(t)P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mu)}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mu)}+\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm FVc}^{\lambda}{P_{V_{s},M(0)+1}\left(\mu\right)}\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mu)\mathrm{d}s}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{P_{\xi_{0},M(0)}(\mu)\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mu)},

where we used the assumption of Lemma
6.4
in the last equality.

As the set of test functions
\left\{P_{f,n}\left|\phantom{1^{1^{1}}}\hskip-14.22636pt\right.f\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{4}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}),n\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}\right\}
is
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})\right)-
convergence determining

[
26
,
Lemma 2.1.2]
, it is
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})\right)-
separating

[
40
,
Chapter 3, Section 4, p.112]
, so we have for any bounded continuous function
\phi
from
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})
to
{\mathbb{R}}
that

\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{P_{\rm FVc}(t)\phi(\mu)\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mu)}=\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{\phi(\mu)\gamma(\mathrm{d}\mu)}.

Hence
\gamma
is an invariant probability measure
for the centered
Fleming-Viot
process with resampling rate
\lambda
. Now, from
[
18
,
Theorem 4.1]
,
\pi^{\lambda}
is its unique invariant probability measure which ends the proof of Lemma
6.4
.

7
Characterisation of the limiting values of the slow component

Combining the results of Sections
5
and
6
, we have proved that for all
R>0
, the sequence of laws of
\left(\left(z^{K,R},\Gamma^{K,R}\right)\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
is tight in
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})\right)\right)
and for any limiting value
{\mathbb{Q}}
of this sequence, the canonical process
\left(\zeta^{R},\Gamma^{R}\right)
on
{\mathscr{C}}^{0}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)\times{\mathcal{M}}_{m}^{T}\left({\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})\right)
satisfies for all
f\in{\mathscr{C}}^{1}_{b}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
, for all
t\in[0,T]
,
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s.,

f\left(\zeta_{t}^{R}\right)=f\left(\zeta_{0}^{R}\right)+\int_{0}^{t}{\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm SLOW}f\left(\zeta_{s}^{R},\mu\right)\pi^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)\mathrm{d}s}}.

(61)

From now on, we will establish in Section
7.1
with Lemma
7.1
that the sequence of laws of
\left(z^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
converges weakly in
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
to the solution of an ODE in the torus. Finally, Section
7.2
allows us to get away from the torus and to prove Theorem
2.1
.

7.1
Convergence of the slow component on the torus

Lemma 7.1
.

The sequence
\left(z^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
converges in law in
{\mathbb{D}}\left([0,T],{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
to the unique solution of

\forall t\in[0,T],\quad z_{t}=x_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}{\partial_{1}{\rm{Fit}}\left(z_{s},z_{s}\right)\beta\left(z_{s}\right)m_{2}\left(z_{s}\right)\mathrm{d}s}

(62)

on the torus
{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
.

Proof.

For any limiting value
{\mathbb{Q}}
of the sequence of laws of
\left(z^{K,R}\right)_{K\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\star}}
,
from (
9
) and applying (
61
) with
f={\rm{id}}\in{\mathscr{C}}_{b}^{2}\left({\mathbb{T}}_{R},{\mathbb{T}}_{R}\right)
, we deduce that
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s.,

\forall t\in[0,T],\quad\zeta_{t}^{R}=\zeta_{0}^{R}+\int_{0}^{t}{\left(\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}({\mathbb{R}})}{M_{2}\left(\mu\right)\pi^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)}(\mathrm{d}\mu)}\right)\partial_{1}{\rm{Fit}}\left(\zeta_{s}^{R},\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)\mathrm{d}s}.

From

[
18
,
Corollary 4.16]
, for all
s\in[0,t]
,

\int_{{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\left({\mathbb{R}}\right)}{M_{2}\left(\mu\right)\pi^{\lambda\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)}\left(\mathrm{d}\mu\right)}=\frac{1}{2\lambda\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)}=\frac{\beta\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)m_{2}\left(\zeta_{s}^{R}\right)}{2},

so
\zeta^{R}
is solution of (
62
)
{\mathbb{Q}}-
a.s. By uniqueness of the solution of (
62
), the announced result follows. ∎

7.2
End of the proof of Theorem
2.1

Let
T>0
be fixed and recall that
x_{0}
is the mean trait value of
\nu_{0}^{K}
. From Lemma
7.1
and since the ODE (
62
) is non-explosive, we can choose
R>0
large enough such that

\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\left(z_{t}^{K,R}\in\left[x_{0}-\frac{R}{2},x_{0}+\frac{R}{2}\right],\forall t\in[0,T]\right)}=1.

From Proposition
4.8
and (
7
), we have that,

\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\left({\rm{Diam}}\left({\rm{Supp\,}}\nu_{t}^{K,R}\right)\leqslant\frac{1}{K^{1+\varepsilon/2}},\forall t\in[0,T]\right)}=1.

Hence,

\lim_{K\to+\infty}{{\mathbb{P}}\left({\rm{Supp\,}}\nu_{t/K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}^{K,R}\subset\left[x_{0}-R,x_{0}+R\right],\forall t\in[0,T]\right)}=1.

Now, on the event
\left\{{\rm{Supp\,}}\nu_{t/K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}^{K,R}\subset\left[x_{0}-R,x_{0}+R\right],\forall t\in[0,T]\right\}
,

\nu_{t/K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}^{K,R}=\nu_{t/K{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sigma_{K}^{2}}}^{K}
for all
t\in[0,T]
, identifying
x\in{\mathbb{T}}_{R}
with its unique representant in
\left[x_{0}-2R,x_{0}+2R\right]
. In particular,

\forall t\in[0,T],\quad z_{t}^{K,R}=z_{t}^{K}.

Theorem
2.1
then follows from Lemma
7.1
.
